## Notes on DP0

- The epic (EP-6, `epc_78NC4FJK`), its 14 milestones and tasks DP-1/DP-2 were minted with `orun task epic|milestone|task create` because the Orunbase MCP connector in the build session was read-only; the result is the same records `epic_create`/`milestone_create`/`task_create` make.
- No `orun/compliance` check reported on #4 (only `plan`); the lineage (branch, trailer, manifest) was written regardless and `task_get DP-1` folded to `done` from the merge.
- The Lumen bootstrap was parked at `03-infrastructure` by the Supabase free-plan project limit (WAITING.md W4). It resumed on 2026-11-23 after the owner freed two slots and finished: phases 03-database → 08-docs landed as #8–#12. Stage and prod are live on `*.nexo-7be.workers.dev` (`/health` 200, database reachable; see `ai/context/deployment.md`). The org is still on the free plan (risks DP-H).

## Notes on DP1

- **Reference build environment (DP-3).** nginx.org's apk signing-key URL, which Coolify's `docker/production/Dockerfile` fetches at build time, answered 404 on 2026-11-23 (upstream `main` has the same line). `tools/parity/reference/up.sh` uses upstream's URL when it answers and otherwise a vendored copy of the same key, checked by sha256 (`ff7cf138…`, byte-identical across archived copies 2019 → 2026-10), with a warning in the run. The Coolify application code and image contents are otherwise exactly `91b7e3e`.
- **Visual job runner** is pinned to `ubuntu-24.04`: `ubuntu-latest` is 26.04, for which Playwright 1.56 ships no Chromium.
- **Reference runs.** The first runs looked green but 96 of 100 "captures" were `/onboarding`: a fresh root team has `show_boarding` on and every page redirects there. The seed now skips onboarding (as "Skip setup" does), and capture records any route that lands somewhere else as `redirected`, never as captured. With that: 7/7 fixtures seeded through `/api/v1`; 91 of 188 visual routes captured at desktop and mobile × light and dark; 88 wait for fixtures (applications, services, databases, backups, sources …) that later DP1 tasks and the feature milestones add; 9 redirect by design (e.g. `/subscription` on a self-hosted instance, `/tags` → `/canvas/tags`). At the time, stage was not deployed; it now is (Lumen console only — the `deploy-web` surface lands in DP3), so no route can pass until DP3.
- **Reference API limits (DP-4).** The contract run sets two operator settings on the reference: `API_RATE_LIMIT=100000` and `API_FAILED_REQUEST_RATE_LIMIT=0`. With the defaults, 299 of 330 unauthenticated checks came back 429: the run was measuring Coolify's rate limiter, not its API. Both limiters are Coolify behaviour that Freeploy must reproduce. They get their own parity scenarios in DP9 (`api.rate-limit`, `api.failed-request-throttle`), measured with the limits on. The runner also retries a 429 honouring `Retry-After`, and fails the run if any answer is still 429.
- **Command goldens (DP-6).** 43 cases over pure `bootstrap/helpers` functions, emitted by Coolify itself in the reference container (run 45107418510) and committed as `tools/parity/commands/goldens.json`. Every run re-emits them and fails on any difference. No helper is ported yet, so all 43 are `pending`.
- **Fixtures (DP-7).** The application, scheduled task, PostgreSQL, Redis, backup schedule, S3 storage, one-click service, cloud-init script, GitHub App and GitLab App fixtures are now created through `/api/v1`, with nothing deployed. Visual routes captured: 169 of 188 (was 91). API reads needing a fixture: 19 (was 52). The 19 routes still waiting need a deployment (DP5), a cloud-provider token (DP11), a Gitea app (no create endpoint), or redirect by design. The id mask now requires letters and digits, after a long word of copy (`excludedcontenttypes`) was found masked; IPv4 addresses are masked as they change per runner. Known limitation: ids embedded inside longer text (Traefik label lines, sslip domains) still differ per run. DP5 makes fixture ids deterministic where the API allows, or masks those spans explicitly.
- **Behaviour and the stage target (DP-8, closes DP1).** `behaviour/run.ts` drives a scenario through `/api/v1` and reads the resulting container state from Docker, normalised (fixture ids, digests, short ids, times) so two systems compare field by field. The reference runs it on its own host, the CI runner, which Coolify manages as `localhost`: first scenario `application.dockerimage.deploy`. The Freeploy side reads its server over SSH and needs a test VM (WAITING.md W1); until then the report says "not run". The harness now runs against Freeploy **stage** (`vars.PARITY_TARGET_URL`, `vars.PARITY_TARGET_API_URL`). Stage serves the Lumen console and no Coolify routes or `/api/v1` yet, so every route and read is reported *missing*. That is the "empty stage" DP1 asked for.
- The parity report is published as a workflow artefact and job summary on every run rather than committed back to `main` by CI (branch protection requires a PR for every change). `parity/README.md` is regenerated by `inventory.ts` in the PRs that change rows.
- **Parity runs locally (DP-76).** To save CI time, the parity workflow no longer runs on pull requests or pushes to main: it is `workflow_dispatch` only, for a full run on demand (rebuilding the reference, a new Coolify commit). Each change instead runs the harness's own checks locally — `npm test` and `inventory.ts --check` in tools/parity, what the `parity · unit` job ran — and the visual/DOM comparison runs locally with `tools/parity/local-stage.sh` against stage after a deploy, reusing a reference capture. `local-stage.sh` now also takes a directory holding a reference capture instead of a CI run id, and the stage user's credentials from `LOCAL_EMAIL`/`LOCAL_PASSWORD` in the environment (the macOS keychain stays the default), so it runs outside a Mac.

## Notes on DP2

- **Schema.** 75 `deploy` tables generated from the folded final schema (500_deploy_core, #17, applied on stage and prod), plus `org_keys` (510) and a generated delta (520). The first fold missed migrations that loop over table lists (`foreach ([…] as $t) Schema::table($t, …)`, in 16 files). With those unrolled, 520 adds 28 columns and drops 16 that Coolify itself dropped. 500 stays frozen because it is applied; the generator now emits only the delta, and a test holds 520 to it.
- **Tenancy.** Row-level security is forced on every table, for role `deploy_app`. `withOrg()` is the only way the worker reaches the data. Isolation is proven for all 76 tables against a real Postgres (PGlite): own read and write, the other org sees and changes nothing, no cross-org writes, no org means no data, the operator sees all.
- **Encryption.** The 85 columns Coolify encrypts on deploy tables (`deploy/encrypted-columns.ts`, generated from the models' casts) are stored as `fp1` AES-256-GCM ciphertexts under a per-org data key. That key is wrapped by the platform KEK `DEPLOY_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (a Worker secret seeded per environment) and bound to its org and column. Five casts are not here: 2 on tables Lumen owns, and 3 that Coolify declares on columns its own schema never creates (`instance_settings.sentinel_token`, `servers.logdrain_*`; the real columns are on `server_settings`).
- **Worker.** `deploy-core-worker` is live on stage and prod behind `api-edge` `/v1/organizations/{org}/deploy/*` (#18). Unauthenticated requests get 401 at the edge.
- **Schema catch-up (DP-60).** The fold reads Blueprint calls, so it misses what a migration does in a raw statement, in a loop over a list held in a variable, inside `if (Schema::hasColumn(…))`, through `->change()`, or to a table named by `config()`; 590, 610 and 630 each patched one such gap by hand. **Method:** Coolify's 531 migrations were run by Laravel on PostgreSQL 16 (all 46 raw statements took effect; none had to be applied by hand) and the catalogs dumped to `packages/db/scripts/coolify-schema/coolify-schema.json` (reproducible byte for byte; its README has the commands); the same catalog queries describe `deploy` on PGlite, and `tests/db/src/deploy-schema-parity.ts` compares the two fact by fact. The report is `parity/schema-diff.md` (generated; `UPDATE_SCHEMA_DIFF=1 pnpm --filter ./tests/db test -- deploy-schema-parity`). **Counts:** 90 Coolify tables, 14 not ported by design (the 13 of `LUMEN_OWNED` and Laravel's `migrations`), 75 compared, 1665 columns. Before 660 there were 213 differences; `660_deploy_schema_catch_up` closes 212: 93 columns added (restart counters, health check and `redact_all_env_values_in_logs` on the eight standalone database tables, `scheduled_tasks.consecutive_failures` / `failing_since` / `disabled_reason`, `servers.digitalocean_droplet_id` / `_status`, `failure_count` on the six notification-settings tables, `worker_host` on the three execution tables, maintenance mode on applications and services, the two auto-cancel switches of `application_settings`), 24 columns changed (the four `manual_webhook_secret_*` and the deployment queue's `configuration_snapshot` / `configuration_diff` to `text`, `uuid NOT NULL` on four tables, `servers.proxy` nullable, the backup retention columns `NOT NULL`, `max_restart_count` default 0 on previews and service applications, and the default images of PostgreSQL, MySQL, MariaDB, MongoDB and ClickHouse, which Coolify raised on 2026-11-06), `environment_variables.is_build_time` dropped as Coolify dropped it, the `skipped` status of `scheduled_task_executions`, 46 indexes, 33 unique indexes and Coolify's fillfactor / autovacuum options on 14 tables. **Unique indexes:** 29 keep Coolify's columns (`team_id` as `org_id`) because what they are made of is already an org's row; four made of a name alone get `org_id` in front (`application_previews.fqdn`, `image_scan_policies`, `notification_throttles`, `scheduled_job_deliveries`); the report argues each one's scope and why no existing row can violate it — `main`, which stage and prod run, writes none of those tables yet, and the services that will write them refuse duplicates first. **Left:** `activity_log` (Coolify's store of command output; the fold never saw it; where Freeploy keeps command output is decided with the SSH executor, W3). By design and excluded by rule: no foreign-key constraints (62 in Coolify; every referencing column is indexed), `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` where Coolify froze `now()` (14 columns), `uuid` unique on two tables where Coolify's is not. Two DP2 choices the comparison surfaced and did not change: `oauth_settings (provider)` and `scheduled_job_states (schedule_key)` are unique across orgs. **Guard:** `tests/db/src/deploy-schema-parity.test.ts` fails, naming the table, when Coolify's dump has a column, index, unique index or CHECK value that `deploy` lacks, and the isolation test now runs on the schema after 660. **660 applies to stage and prod on merge.** **What can now rely on the database** (not rewritten here): the look-ups before a write that stand in for a unique index — `packages/db/src/deploy/environments.ts:116` with `apps/deploy-core-worker/src/projects/service.ts:256` and `:285` (environments); `apps/deploy-core-worker/src/shared-variables/service.ts:257` and `:346` (shared variables); `apps/deploy-core-worker/src/applications/storages.ts:257` and `:263`, and `applications/resource-operations.ts:179` (volume names, mount paths); `applications/tags.ts:104` and `:119` (a tag attached twice); `destinations/service.ts:203` (a network twice on a server); `packages/db/src/deploy/additional-destinations.ts:114` (update-else-insert, now expressible as `ON CONFLICT`) — each could map SQLSTATE 23505 to Coolify's "Duplicate entry found." and stop racing; `applications/scheduled-tasks.ts:104` returns `disabledReason: null` because the column did not exist; the deployment queue's snapshot columns can now hold `fp1` ciphertexts, but `deploy/encrypted-columns.ts` does not list them (Coolify casts them with `EncryptedArrayCast`, which the generator's `encrypted*` match misses). **Behaviour that changes with 660:** a MySQL, MariaDB, MongoDB or ClickHouse database created without an image gets Coolify's current default (`mysql:9.7`, `mariadb:12.3`, `mongo:9`, `clickhouse/clickhouse-server:26.8`); `tests/db/src/deploy-databases.test.ts:265` and the fake store in `tests/deploy-core-worker/src/databases.test.ts:107` still describe the defaults of 500–630.
- **Running on the caught-up schema (DP-63).** Until now most `tests/db/src/deploy-*.test.ts` suites read a fixed list of migration files (500, 510, 520, sometimes 590 / 610 / 630), so the feature SQL was tested on an older schema than the one stage and prod run. **Moved:** `tests/db/src/deploy-schema.ts` loads every deploy migration of the manifest in manifest order (the migrations themselves create `deploy_app` and the forced row-level security, so nothing else is set up), with the executor and the test KEK the suites each had a copy of; all 21 deploy suites run on it. Four keep an explicit list, each for a test about one migration step, on a database of its own: `deploy-shared-variables` (590 widens the type check), `deploy-application-resources` (610 makes tag names per org), `deploy-databases` (630 adds `public_port_timeout`) and `deploy-schema-parity` (660 on top of 630, and the report's before / after); each of the first three also asserts the same fact at head. The isolation suite takes its table list from the manifest's migrations instead of a list of its own. No assertion was removed; tests/db went from 966 to 986 tests. **What the move found.** No statement of `packages/db/src/deploy` fails on the head schema: every insert already gives a uuid, nothing reads `is_build_time`, nothing reads or writes the queue's snapshot columns, and every reader of `servers.proxy` takes null (now asserted). What was wrong: (1) *test fixtures only* — two raw inserts without a uuid (`environment_variables` in `deploy-application-create`, `local_persistent_volumes` in `deploy-deployments`), which 660 refuses; (2) *the tests were stale, not the code* — `deploy-databases.test.ts` and the fake store of `tests/deploy-core-worker/src/databases.test.ts` expected `mongo:7`, `mysql:8`, `mariadb:11`, `clickhouse/clickhouse-server:25.11` (and the fake `postgres:16-alpine`); Coolify at 91b7e3e stores `mongo:9`, `mysql:9.7`, `mariadb:12.3`, `clickhouse/clickhouse-server:26.8`, `postgres:18-alpine` (its migration of 2026-11-06), and 660 has those. `engines.ts` mirrors no default image and DP-57's PHP goldens do not cover schema defaults (their SQLite tables have none, as their README says), so neither changed; (3) *a real defect, the one DP-60 named* — `addServer` attaches any network of a usable server, the application's main one too (Coolify's `Destination::addServer()` does the same; the page never offers it), and `promoteServer` then inserted the main destination a second time: on stage, after 660, the pivot's unique index refuses that and the request answered 503. Coolify hits the same index, rolls its transaction back and shows "Duplicate entry found. Please use a different name."; `promoteServer` now asks first and answers that (422) with nothing changed. As in Coolify the row then stays (`removeServer` refuses the main server first); (4) *a real defect on every look-up-then-write path* — two requests at the same moment both pass the look-up, and the second one's write is now refused by an index: a 503 on stage where Coolify shows its duplicate message. `packages/db/src/deploy/unique-indexes.ts` names the ten indexes of 660 on tables the service layer writes (`environments`, `local_persistent_volumes`, `local_file_volumes`, `standalone_dockers`, `swarm_dockers`, `taggables`, `additional_destinations`, three on `shared_environment_variables`) and `refusingDuplicates()` gives a write refused by one of them (SQLSTATE 23505, by index name) the look-up's own answer: 409 for an environment's name and a shared variable's key on rename and in the developer view, 422 "Variable already exists." on add, 422 for a volume name, a mount path, a tag ("Duplicate tags."), a network ("Network already added to this server.") and a promotion; another index's violation or any other error is still a 503. `attachAdditionalDestination` is `ON CONFLICT … DO NOTHING`, so a network attached twice at once is attached once; (5) *a wrong answer* — a scheduled task's `disabledReason` was the constant null because the column did not exist; it is now `scheduled_tasks.disabled_reason`, enabling a disabled task clears `consecutive_failures`, `failing_since` and the reason as `ScheduledTask`'s saving hook does, and the task page shows Coolify's "Disabled because it keeps failing" callout. Nothing writes a reason until the scheduler exists; (6) *the generator* — `generate-deploy-schema.ts` matched casts that start with `encrypted` and so missed Coolify's own cast class, `EncryptedArrayCast` (`Crypt::encryptString` of the JSON), on `application_deployment_queues.configuration_snapshot` and `configuration_diff`. `encrypted-columns.ts` now lists them: 90 encrypting casts in Coolify's models (87 `encrypted`, 1 `encrypted:array`, 2 `EncryptedArrayCast`), 5 not on deploy tables, **85 listed** (83 before; the 86 this file said was never the generated number), and the coverage test also holds every listed column to a text type at head. Nothing writes those two columns yet. **Tests:** `tests/db/src/deploy-unique-indexes.test.ts` (per index: the store function the service asks finds the duplicate, the write that did not ask is refused with 23505 naming the index and leaves nothing, the list of names is exactly 660's indexes on those tables; the promotion sequence on PGlite) and, with memory stores, the HTTP answers in `tests/deploy-core-worker` (projects, shared variables, destinations, storages, tags, additional servers). **Not proven here:** a service running against PGlite end to end — tests/db cannot load the worker and tests/deploy-core-worker has no Postgres — so the two halves meet at the index names, which both suites read from the same constant. **Left:** org-wide shared variables have no index (their three owner columns are NULL, in Coolify too), so there the look-up is the only guard and two simultaneous adds store the key twice; two simultaneous requests that create the same new tag name still end in a 503 (`tags (org_id, name)`, 610), as do a duplicate uuid and the `application_previews`, `image_scan_policies`, `notification_throttles` and `scheduled_job_deliveries` indexes, whose writers do not exist yet; the look-ups themselves stay (they give the answer its words) rather than becoming `ON CONFLICT`; the deployment queue's configuration snapshot has no writer (DP-56 deferred it) and must encrypt when it gets one; `scheduled_tasks.consecutive_failures` / `failing_since`, the databases' restart counters and health-check columns, `worker_host`, maintenance mode and the auto-cancel switches are in the schema and unread. No migration was needed: 660 is right as it is.


## Notes on DP3

- **Porting method.** A page starts from the reference's rendered markup (the parity capture's `desktop-light.html`), turned into JSX by `tools/parity/port/html2tsx.ts`; `tools/parity/port/gen-deploy-web.sh` regenerates every generated file from a capture. Fixture values in the markup (the user's name, the team, the current page) are bound to props by a patch file per markup (`tools/parity/port/patches/*.json`); every rule must match, so a reference change that moves the markup stops the generator instead of leaving a fixture value in the product. Where state changes the markup (the Appearance cards' selected state), the page is ported by hand from the Blade source instead.
- **Signed-in layout (DP-17).** All of what Coolify's layouts put in `<body>` is generated as one component (`LayoutMarkup`): the shell, the reminders, the command palette and the create dialogs (hidden), because the DOM gate counts every form control on the page. Dropdowns open and close as Alpine's do; the command palette, the create dialogs, the team switcher's actions and the mobile sheet are markup only until their own tasks.
- **No notification channels yet.** Coolify shows "No notifications enabled" when the team has none; Freeploy has none until DP9, so the reminder always shows until dismissed for the month, as on the reference.
- **Personal team.** Registration gives the user a team named as Coolify names it, "<name>'s Team". A DP-15 bug created the team under the internal user id; it is now created under the public id, and on every password sign-in as on the other sign-in paths (live on stage: a new user gets "Parity User's Team").
- **Who the capture signs in as (DP-18).** Until DP-17 the reference capture signed in as Coolify's instance root user. A Freeploy tenant is a regular user: their own personal team, no instance settings, an account they can delete. The seed now creates "Parity User" (user@example.com) with its own team, a `localhost` server on the runner (the root team's host and SSH key, copied into the team) and an API token for that team, and the fixtures and the capture use it. Root stays the instance admin, for the DP12 platform-admin pages. The stage counterpart is the same name and address, its password only in `PARITY_TARGET_PASSWORD`. An earlier stage parity user (root@example.com, DP-17) is unused.
- **Edge CORS (DP-18).** The edge allowed only the Lumen console's origin, so a browser on deploy-web could never read sign-in's answer; checks made with curl did not notice, because CORS is a browser rule. deploy-web's workers.dev origin is now allowed per environment.
- **Profile (DP-19).** Name and password work; Livewire's `dispatch('success'|'error')` toasts are Coolify's toast stack (`layout/Toasts.tsx`), and a ValidationException's message is summarised as Laravel does ("… (and 1 more error)"). The danger zone renders Coolify's blocker branch for the team, with deletion itself a later task; the email-change dialog, avatar, 2FA and passkeys render and are later tasks. The reference used to run on http://127.0.0.1, where Coolify offers no passkeys, while stage is HTTPS; since DP-22 it is reached as `localhost`, where Coolify offers them, as stage does.
- **First signed-in pages through the gate (DP-21's run).** `/profile` and `/profile/appearance` pass on stage at desktop and mobile, light and dark, pixels and DOM; `/login`, `/register` and `/forgot-password` still pass. `/two-factor-required` (a team that requires 2FA) is not ported yet.
- **Passkeys (DP-22).** WebAuthn by `@simplewebauthn/server` on the identity worker and `@simplewebauthn/browser` in deploy-web, in place of Coolify's `@laravel/passkeys`; the relying party is deploy-web's host (from `DEPLOY_WEB_URL`); the WebAuthn challenge travels sealed in the request, so there is no challenge table. Registration needs a confirmed password (Fortify passkeys confirmPassword); deletion asks for the password as Coolify's modal does (the full three-step `<x-modal-confirmation>` comes with the component gallery). A passkey sign-in skips 2FA.
- **Two-factor authentication (DP-20).** Fortify's flow with Coolify's options (confirm, confirmPassword): `/v1/auth/password/confirm` (3 hours, per session), enable → confirm with a code → recovery codes, regenerate, disable, and a challenge at password sign-in (by code or recovery code; a used recovery code is replaced). TOTP is RFC 6238 (SHA-1, 6 digits, 30 s, one step of drift, verified against the RFC's vectors) and a code is never accepted twice, as `verifyKeyNewer` does. The secret, the codes and the sign-in challenge are sealed with AES-256-GCM under `IDENTITY_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (a Worker secret seeded per environment, never printed), bound to the user. The screens are DP-21. Verified end to end on stage through the API with a throwaway fake user (qa-2fa-…@example.com, left on stage): 423 until the password is confirmed, Fortify's messages for a wrong password, a bad code and a bad recovery code, setup confirmed with a real TOTP code, a challenge instead of a session at sign-in, a replayed code refused, a recovery code accepted once.
- **Two-factor screens (DP-21).** `/user/confirm-password` is generated from the reference. The /profile section's pending and enabled states and `/two-factor-challenge` are hand-ported from their Blade views, because the capture sees only the "off" state and the challenge needs a pending sign-in; the QR code is Fortify's (192 px, slate on white) drawn with `qrcode-generator`.
- **Unported routes (DP-23).** A route not ported yet sends a signed-out visitor to /login and shows a signed-in user the layout with a notice, instead of an empty page (stage showed a black screen at `/`).
- **Email verification and change (DP-24).** Coolify only enforces verification in Cloud mode; Freeploy is a hosted service, so it applies: a new password account gets Coolify's signed link (60 minutes, `/email/verify/{id}/{sha256(email)}?expires&signature`, signed with HMAC under `IDENTITY_ENCRYPTION_KEY`) and only reaches /verify until it opens it. Accounts that existed before migration 570, and every magic-link or OAuth sign-in, count as verified (those channels prove the address). The email change follows `Profile\Index` exactly: three limiters (1 per 2 minutes per user, 3 per hour per new address, 5 per hour per IP), a 6-digit code hashed at rest and valid 10 minutes, resend after half the expiry, and the request dropped after 10 failed attempts. The inventory now counts a controller action that returns a view as a page (`/verify`, `/auth/link`, the invitation page), which it previously skipped.
- **Custom theme (DP-25).** Coolify's colour engine from `layouts/base.blade.php` (OKLab/OKLCH, WCAG contrast, the 12 presets, the share code) is a module, `lib/custom-theme.ts`; it reproduces Coolify's own default accent value exactly (`#6b16ed` → `oklch(49.65% 0.2709 289.33)`). The dialog is ported from the Blade; while it is closed the page keeps the captured hidden markup the gate compares.
- **Account deletion (DP-25).** Coolify's self-hosted rule, the reference's: a team where the user is the only member must be emptied of projects, servers and Git sources first (Cloud instead deletes them with the account). The account is deactivated and anonymised (credentials, passkeys, 2FA, OAuth links and avatar deleted; sessions revoked; the address freed), not removed, because sessions and security events reference it. Coolify deletes a single-member team; Freeploy cannot delete an organisation yet (DP10), so the copy says the team is left without members instead of claiming it is deleted. The confirmation asks for the email address and the password; the full three-step `<x-modal-confirmation>` comes with the first page that needs it (risks DP-M).
- **Account deletion's resource check under row-level security (DP-77).** Account deletion (DP-25) refuses while the user's team still holds projects, servers or Git sources (`Team::isEmpty()`), counted by identity's `orgResourceCount`. That count was a plain query on `deploy.*`, whose tables force row-level security: it saw the rows only if the connection's role bypasses RLS. Every worker connects through one Hyperdrive as Supabase's `postgres` role; whether that role has BYPASSRLS on stage and prod was not checked (no database access from the build session), so the guard either worked by that accident or never refused. The count now runs inside `withOrg()` (as `deploy_app` with the org set), so it sees exactly that org's rows whatever role the worker connects as. A tests/db suite counts from a session subject to RLS: the old query counts 0 there, the new one 2.
- **Avatar (DP-25).** Coolify's flow: the browser crops to a square and compresses to a 256 px JPEG, the server checks `image|mimes:jpg,jpeg,png,webp|max:5120|dimensions:max 6000`. Freeploy has no object storage bound yet, so the image is kept in Postgres (migration 580) and served by the edge at `/v1/auth/avatar/{usr_…}`.
- **Two-factor required (DP-25).** `/two-factor-required` is generated from the reference. The reference escapes the team name twice ("Parity User&#039;s Team", a Coolify bug); Freeploy shows the apostrophe, a known, listed difference.
- **Component gallery: deferred** by the owner on 2026-11-24 (risks DP-M). Coolify has no gallery page to compare with; components are compared on the pages that render them.
- **DP3 closed (DP-26).** Routes through the gate on stage (CI's pixel gate; DOM and copy also checked from a workstation after the last deploy): `/login`, `/register`, `/forgot-password`, `/profile/appearance` pass; `/user/confirm-password`, `/two-factor-required`, `/verify`, `/profile` were fixed in DP-26 to the remaining causes below. Every difference left is listed with its cause:
  - `/profile` danger zone: the reference's team holds the fixture project and server, so Coolify shows its "Delete all projects…" blocker; Freeploy's team holds nothing until fixtures can be applied to Freeploy (projects and servers, DP4/DP5; `/api/v1`, DP9).
  - `/two-factor-required`: Coolify escapes the team name twice ("Parity User&#039;s Team"); Freeploy shows the apostrophe.
  - `/login` "Passkeys are not supported in this browser.": shown by Coolify's `@laravel/passkeys` (and by Freeploy) in browsers without passkey autofill, such as the headless Chromium CI uses; in a browser with autofill neither shows it.
  - `/reset-password/{token}` is captured only with a fixture token; it renders on both.
  - Email is not delivered on stage yet (WAITING W5), so verification is not enforced there (`REQUIRE_EMAIL_VERIFICATION=false`) and the email-change code and reset links cannot be completed by a person on stage; the flows are covered by tests and the API checks above.
- **Account rate limits (DP-26).** Every `/v1/auth/*` call used the edge's tight `auth` limit (10 a minute), and one /profile load now makes about six of them, so a user was refused within a minute. Calls on a signed-in user's own account (session, profile, 2FA state, passkey list, email-change state, deletion info, avatar) now use an `account` family at the ordinary 60 a minute; every step that checks a secret (sign-in, registration, reset, password confirm and change, 2FA confirm and challenge, passkey sign-in, email-change code, account deletion, OAuth) keeps the tight limit.
- **Faster parity (DP-25).** CI reuses the reference capture of an earlier successful run when nothing that shapes the reference changed (the Coolify commit, the harness, the inventory), so a PR only captures and compares Freeploy. Until Freeploy serves `/api/v1` (DP9) nothing else needs a live reference; from DP9, `PARITY_FULL` forces a rebuild. `tools/parity/local-stage.sh <run>` compares stage on a workstation against a CI run's reference capture (the stage user's password comes from the macOS keychain).
- **The dashboard and an environment's Clone page (DP-100).** Two pages deploy-web lacked, hand-ported from their Blade views (no reference capture of either with data), on existing rows; no binding, dependency or migration. **Dashboard** (`/`, route `dashboard`, `pages/Dashboard.tsx`, `lib/dashboard.ts`; `/` was the "not ported" notice): `Dashboard::mount()` / `render()` — the team's projects (Project\Index's counts: environments and the eleven resource relations) and servers, each section a table or a grid of the first `ITEM_LIMIT` (8) of the sorted list with "View all" past eight, `sortItems()` (by name, then the column, SORT_NATURAL \| SORT_FLAG_CASE as `strnatcasecmp()`, stable, descending when asked) behind `<x-table.sort-header>`'s Project / Contents (first desc) / Description and Server / Description / Status, `isValidSort()`; a project opens `navigateTo()` (its only environment, else the project), with Add resource (its first environment's `/new`) and Project settings; the New project dialog (`AddEmpty`), New server; the empty states that start a team — "No projects yet", "A private key is required" (Add private key) before the first server, then "No servers yet" (New server); `<x-canvas-view-toggle>` (table / grid, and the Canvas link to `/dashboard/canvas`; only the Canvas link while there are no projects); `serverStatus()` — Disabled, Unavailable, Unreachable, Not ready, Attention required (`proxySet()` with the proxy not running or `hasCurrentTraefikOutdatedInfo()`, or Sentinel enabled and out of sync), Ready — as the grid card's warning icon and the table's dot; and `<livewire:dashboard.active-deployments>` polled every 3 s (the org's applications' deployments, soft-deleted ones included as `applicationIdsOwnedByTeam()` does, five queued or in progress by status then id and five others newest first, `$deploymentStatusMeta` with Blocked for an image scan's block, "Pull request #n", the environment path, diffForHumans and toDayDateTimeString). The table / grid choice of each section is remembered in this browser (`freeploy.view.dashboard_projects|servers`; `?view=table|grid` first). **`/dashboard`** is no route of Coolify's: its catch-all `Route::any('/{any}')` sends a signed-in user to `RouteServiceProvider::HOME` (`/`), so deploy-web does the same (`dashboard.fallback`), and DP-98's dashboard canvas "List view" now leads to `/?view=list` (`route('dashboard')`), its breadcrumb to `/`. **Clone** (`/project/{uuid}/environment/{env_uuid}/clone`, route `project.clone-me`, `pages/EnvironmentClone.tsx`, `lib/clone-me.ts`; EnvironmentEdit's "Clone environment" link now opens it): `CloneMe::mount()` — the environment, the default name `str(clone_resource_name($project->name, new_public_id()))->slug()`, the servers that are not build servers by id each with its standalone then Swarm destinations (one selected; `selectServer()` clears a choice clicked again; "n destination(s)"), the applications, databases and services each by name (PHP's regular comparison) with "n resource(s) will be cloned."; and `clone($type)` — anything but `project` clones into an environment, as the method's `else` does — in Coolify's order: `validate()` with `CloneMe::messages()` ("Please select a server & destination.", "Please enter a name for the new project or environment.") and, since `nameMessages()` is keyed `name.*`, Laravel's own for newName ("The new name field must be at least 3 characters.", "… must not be greater than 255 characters.", "… format is invalid.", "… must be a string."), every failing rule of a field; "Environment not found."; "Destination not found." (`findProjectTeamDestination()`: the org's, on a server that can host resources); then one transaction: a new project ("Project with the same name already exists."; description `… (clone)`; its production environment, plus the source environment's name when that is not production) or a new environment of the project ("Environment with the same name already exists."; stored lower-cased by `customizeName()`, so a name differing only in case meets the unique index: "Duplicate entry found. Please use a different name."), then every application through DP-55's `cloneApplicationTo()` (clone_application(): `clone-of-…`, settings, tags, scheduled tasks, volumes, mounts, variables) and every standalone database through DP-64's `cloneDatabaseTo()` with the source's name kept (the database branch's `replicate()` fills no name); the page then opens the new environment. Each failure is handleError()'s toast (a validation error summarised as Laravel does). **What answers 501, with the transaction rolled back so nothing is stored**: an environment with a service — "Cloning a service is not available yet." (clone_service() is not ported, DP-97) — and, after it in Coolify's order (what `DB::afterCommit()` and `ServerStorageSaveJob::dispatch()->afterCommit()` queue, resource by resource, applications then databases, the volume copy before the file mounts), "Copying the volume data to the clone needs the SSH executor, which is not available yet." (Clone volume data with a volume to copy) and "Writing the clone's file mounts to the server needs the SSH executor, which is not available yet." (a managed mount inside a clone's directory). The confirmation for Clone volume data is Coolify's (`<x-modal-confirmation>` without text or password). **Endpoints** (deploy-core-worker; `deploy.resource.read` / `write`, a denial 404 that runs nothing, inside `withOrg`): `GET /v1/organizations/{org}/deploy/dashboard` (projects, servers with `status`/`statusType`/`isMetricsEnabled`, `hasPrivateKeys`, `canCreate`/`canUpdate` as the write) and `GET …/dashboard/deployments` (`handlers/dashboard.ts`, `dashboard/service.ts`, a new route of the router); `GET` and `POST …/deploy/projects/{uuid}/environments/{env_uuid}/clone` (`handlers/environment-clone.ts`, `projects/clone-me.ts`, routed before handleProjects), both asking the write first (`can.create.resources`, update on the project, placeResource and the sources' deploy / manage / stop are all the org-wide write here). *Data*: `packages/db` `deploy/dashboard.ts` (`listDashboardServers`, `orgHasPrivateKeys`, `listDashboardDeployments`, `projectNameExists`). *Departures*: Coolify's GET of an environment outside the project renders an error (mount() leaves it null); here it is a 404 and the page goes to the dashboard, as `CanCreateResources` describes; members limited to some projects (`showsTeamSections`) do not exist in Freeploy; the dashboard never redirects to the canvas (RemembersCanvasView is not ported, as in DP-98); the "You are not allowed to create projects in this team." check is the same write; the audit log entry (`ui.project.clone_started`) and the secret manager links are not written. *Cut*: on the dashboard, the pending team invitations (no endpoint lists a user's invitations to other teams), the security update callout (image scanning), the starred resources (no stars table), the traffic analytics section and the servers' metrics charts (Sentinel's data needs the SSH executor; the Usage column shows "-"); on Clone, a service (above). Tests: `tests/deploy-core-worker/src/dashboard.test.ts` (serverStatus()'s rules, the page, the deployments, authorization, org isolation, routing) and `environment-clone.test.ts` (over the API goldens' fixture in PGlite: mount(), every rule and message in order, both clone types and their rows, the three 501s storing nothing, authorization, org isolation, routing); `tests/db/src/deploy-dashboard.test.ts`; `apps/deploy-web/test/dashboard.test.ts` and `routes.test.ts`. Not rendered in a browser, not run against the reference or on stage.
## Notes on DP4

**Where DP4 stands (2026-11-25, DP-43).** Done: the command helpers with their goldens (DP-28), the Go `ssh-executor` and its client (DP-30), the `SshSession` Durable Object, held back from deploy (DP-31), the SSH key library (DP-33), private keys (DP-36), servers: list, add, general, private key, danger (DP-37), the server settings pages (DP-42), destinations (DP-44), and the layout's sidebar submenus (DP-41). The deploy API was unreachable for a signed-in user until DP-38–DP-40; it is now verified on stage end to end (generate, rename and delete a key; create, list and delete a server). Left in DP4, in order:
1. The server pages that need no SSH: CA certificate, resources, registries, cloud-provider token, and the server sidebar as Coolify's per-resource menu (`navLevel`).
2. Everything that runs on a server, once W3 lands: restore the `containers` entry and `SSH_SESSION` binding, then validate and install (`ValidateAndInstallServerJob` as a Workflow), check connection, proxy (Traefik/Caddy) start, stop, configuration, dynamic config and logs, Sentinel push and metrics, Docker cleanup runs, log drains, creating, scanning and removing destination networks, and the server and container terminal over PTY.
3. The end-to-end gate on a test VM (W1): validated, Docker installed, proxy running, terminal opens; then the stage parity fixture gets a validated server so `/servers`, `/security/private-key` and `/profile` match the reference's data.

- **Command layer (DP-28).** 26 of Coolify's `bootstrap/helpers` functions (docker.php, proxy.php, remoteProcess.php, with `SensitiveDataRedactor::redactText` for `remove_iip`) are TypeScript in `apps/deploy-core-worker/src/helpers/`, and every command golden passes: 70 of 70 cases, Coolify's own PHP output byte for byte. PHP semantics that change output are reproduced explicitly: `escapeshellarg`, PHP's `trim` character list, truthiness of `"0"`, `$` matching before a final newline, `(int)` overflow, `version_compare`, `json_decode`'s failure cases, PHP's float printing, heredoc indentation, and a pure-TypeScript md5 (Workers-safe). Known gaps, none reached by a golden: PCRE's backtrack limit (the redactor's "suppressed" fallback cannot occur in JavaScript), numeric-string key order in `json_decode` objects, and `dockerStopCommand`'s Server-model form (the executor passes the version).

- **ssh-executor (DP-30).** A Go service (`golang.org/x/crypto/ssh`) for a Cloudflare Container, doing what Coolify does with the `ssh` binary: the batch runs through Coolify's `remoteShellCommand()` with the commands on stdin (`sshBatchInput`), key authentication only, no agent, forwarding or TTY, ConnectTimeout 10 s, ServerAliveInterval 20 s, a 3600 s command timeout (exit 124 like `timeout`), and host keys trusted on first use then pinned, with Coolify's HOST_KEY_CHANGED_MESSAGE (brand swapped) when a key changes. Output streams as NDJSON. Tested in Go against an in-process SSH server (set -e, stderr, exit codes, pinning, timeout, upload with mode). `deploy-core-worker/src/executor.ts` is the client with `instant_remote_process`'s semantics (trimmed output, `"null"` → null, `sanitize_utf8_text`, `excludeCertainErrors`' messages). Departure: Coolify multiplexes connections per server for an hour (ControlPersist); Freeploy will keep one connection per `SshSession` Durable Object instead (next task).

- **SshSession (DP-31).** One Durable Object per server (`idFromName(serverId)`), extending `@cloudflare/containers` with the executor's image; it sleeps after ten idle minutes. It replaces Coolify's per-server SSH multiplexing (one ControlMaster kept for an hour). The first Container deploy (stage) was refused by the Cloudflare API (`/accounts/{id}/containers/me`), so the container entry and binding are held back until WAITING W3 is resolved; the class (migration v1) is deployed everywhere without a container.

- **SSH keys (DP-33).** `deploy-core-worker/src/keys/ssh-keys.ts` re-implements what Coolify does with phpseclib, on Web Crypto only: unencrypted OpenSSH and PEM (PKCS#1, SEC1, PKCS#8) keys for Ed25519, ECDSA nistp256/384/521 and RSA; Coolify's five validation messages in its order; the OpenSSH public key; phpseclib's fingerprints (sha256: base64 without padding or prefix; md5: lowercase hex with colons, from phpseclib's `Fingerprint` trait); generation in phpseclib's byte layout (Ed25519 OpenSSH with phpseclib's default comment, RSA 4096 PKCS#1 with CRLF lines, public key comment `coolify-generated-ssh-key`, kept byte-identical because it is written to servers). 70 tests compare public keys and fingerprints with `ssh-keygen` and feed generated keys back to it. Known differences: no PuTTY Private-MAC check, the XML/JWK/MSBLOB formats phpseclib also reads are refused as invalid, and Coolify's explicit empty comment (`"ssh-ed25519 AAAA… "` with a trailing space) is produced only when the empty comment is passed.

- **Private keys (DP-36).** Coolify's Keys & Tokens → Private Keys: `/v1/organizations/{org}/deploy/private-keys` in deploy-core-worker (list, add with Coolify's ValidationPatterns rules and messages word for word, generate Ed25519/RSA, show with "Used by", rename or replace the key, delete unless in use, clean up unused keys), every call inside `withOrg` with policy `deploy.resource.read`/`write`; the key is stored formatted and encrypted under the org's key (`private_keys.private_key`) with its fingerprint, never returned. Uuids are Coolify's 24-character public ids. `/security/private-key` follows the reference capture; Coolify at 91b7e3e has no separate key page (`/security/private-key/{uuid}` redirects to the index and the editor is a dialog), and Freeploy does the same. Departures: rotation (writes the new key to servers over SSH) and revealing the stored key (needs a password confirmation deploy-core cannot check yet) answer a clear "not available yet"; random key names use Coolify's format with a shorter word list; the generation rate limit and the on-disk key copy are not ported.
- **Servers (DP-37).** Coolify's Servers without SSH: `/v1/organizations/{org}/deploy/servers` in deploy-core-worker (list with Index's status, the add form's options, add by IP or domain with ByIp's rules and messages word for word, General with Show's rules and messages, the server role with Show's confirmation and build-server check, the server's private key, delete), every call inside `withOrg` with policy `deploy.resource.read`/`write`. A new server is stored unvalidated as Coolify stores it before validation: IPs normalized as `Server::normalizeIp` does (IPv6 in `inet_ntop` form), `proxy` `{"redirect_enabled":true,"status":"exited","type":"TRAEFIK"}`, and its `deploy.server_settings` row (role, build flag, image scanning); a changed address keeps `ip_previous` and forgets the SSH host key. Pages: `/servers`, `/servers/new`, `/servers/new/manual`, `/server/{uuid}`, `/server/{uuid}/private-key` and `/server/{uuid}/danger` (Coolify's path for `server.delete`), with the server settings menu and the top-bar server switcher. Departures: validating, revalidating and checking the connection answer "not available yet" (501), and a newly chosen private key is stored without Coolify's connection check and rollback; the duplicate SSH endpoint check covers the org only (Coolify checks every team; across tenants it would reveal another org's server); deleting asks for the server name but not the account password (deploy-core cannot check it yet), deletes the server and its settings at once (Coolify soft-deletes, then a job cleans up), and has no resource, cloud provider or SSH key options because none of those exist yet; creating a server does not add Coolify's default destination, the `COOLIFY_SERVER_*` shared variables or a Sentinel token, and has no server limit; image scanning defaults to off (the team default is not ported); the list is the table view only (no canvas, grid, metrics or patch column) and counts 0 resources until destinations land; cloud provider provisioning and linking, transfers (Enable management), the localhost and Lima variants, the proxy HUD and the star toggle are not ported; a role change saves the role only (Coolify's also saves the open form); the timezone list is the runtime's ICU list with UTC rather than PHP's, and Laravel's `url` rule is approximated; the server menu shows in the page (a fixed rail at xl) instead of inside the main sidebar, and its Security and Cloudflare Tunnel links lead to pages not ported yet; the API names keys by uuid (`private_key_uuid`) rather than numeric id; "Coolify" in the copy reads "Freeploy".
- **Sidebar submenus (DP-41).** The layout's sidebar now opens the submenu that holds the current page, as Coolify's navbar does: `src/layout/sidebar.ts` lists each submenu's children with their `request()->is(...)` patterns from navbar.blade.php (e.g. `security/private-key*` → Keys & Tokens, `team`, `team/members*` → Team), and the first submenu with a matching child is the active one. That panel shows with `data-active-submenu`, its parent entry is `menu-item-active`, the main menu hides, and a child matching its patterns is current (a key's page marks Private Keys). A parent entry opens its submenu and Back opens the main menu without leaving the page, adding `nav-swap-forward`/`nav-swap-back` to every menu list as Alpine's `navSwap` does; the desktop sidebar and the mobile drawer keep their own state. The bindings are patch rules in `tools/parity/port/patches/AppShellMarkup.json` (the drawer's copy is the one followed by the desktop sidebar's list). Departures: only the submenus the reference renders are mapped (a non-admin's: no Settings submenu, no Team → Admin View); the resource menus (`navLevel`, `data-nav-level`) are not ported; the drawer never opens yet, so its reset on close is not ported either.
- **Server settings (DP-42).** Coolify's server settings pages that only read and write `deploy.server_settings`: `GET`/`PATCH /v1/organizations/{org}/deploy/servers/{uuid}/{advanced,docker-cleanup,log-drains,swarm,sentinel}` in deploy-core-worker (`PATCH` is the component's `submit()`, or `instantSave()` with `instantSave: true`), plus `POST …/docker-cleanup/run`, `…/sentinel/restart`, `…/sentinel/regenerate-token` and `…/sentinel/restore-defaults`; every call inside `withOrg` with policy `deploy.resource.read`/`write`, and Sentinel (Coolify's `viewSentinel`, team admin) needs `write` even to read. The components' `#[Validate]` rules with Laravel's default messages (the Livewire property as the attribute), their own copy word for word (the cron checks, one log drain at a time, Swarm deprecated for new teams), `ValidHostname` and `ValidFluentBitConfig` ported. `sentinel_token`, `logdrain_custom_config`, `logdrain_newrelic_license_key` and `logdrain_axiom_api_key` are stored with the per-org cipher (fp1, bound to `server_settings.<column>`); the keys and the Fluent Bit config are returned in plaintext only to writers, as Coolify shows them only to who can update. Pages: `/server/{uuid}/advanced`, `/docker-cleanup`, `/log-drains`, `/swarm` and `/sentinel`, with their menu items (Swarm shown when the org uses Swarm, as `Team::usesSwarm()`). Departures: what runs on the server answers "not available yet" — Run cleanup and Sentinel's Restart/Sync are 501; a log drain Status change saves the fields but, as Coolify's `toggleLogDrain()` does when starting or stopping the drain fails, keeps the enabled flags as stored and answers 501 with the not-available message, so a drain never reads Enabled while nothing runs; where Sentinel saves and then restarts (save, regenerate token, restore defaults) the save is kept and the answer carries the restart as not available yet; the New Relic and Axiom keys are encrypted although Coolify stores them plain, which caps them at 150 characters (`VARCHAR(255)` holds the ciphertext); the base format rules of every log drain field are checked on every save (Livewire checks them as you type, and its save checks only the enabled drain); a missing Sentinel token is created on the first visit of the Sentinel page (Coolify creates it with the settings row) as 32 random bytes, not an encrypted server uuid; restoring Sentinel's defaults clears its URL (Freeploy has no Sentinel endpoint to generate it from); the Sentinel status is not polled, and the development overrides (debug logging, custom image) and the push error are not shown; the cleanup executions list is its empty state and the stalled-cleanup warning never shows, as nothing runs cleanups yet; the cron validator re-implemented dragonmantank/cron-expression's field rules, which accept more than Coolify (replaced in DP-68); the Swarm notice drops Coolify's v5 plans and migration guide link; Coolify's HTML helper texts are plain text; the menu keeps Coolify's rule that Advanced, Docker Cleanup, Log Drains and Sentinel are disabled until the server is validated, and Log Drains and Sentinel show "Server validation required" until then, so with validation unavailable these pages are reached by URL and only Advanced, Docker Cleanup and Swarm show their forms; the Sentinel menu item now also hides for a Swarm manager (Coolify's `isSwarm()`, DP-37 checked the worker only); "Coolify" in the copy reads "Freeploy" (`/freeploy/production`, `freeploy-sentinel`, `/data/freeploy/log-drains/files`).
- **Destinations (DP-44).** Coolify's Destinations without SSH, over `deploy.standalone_dockers` and `deploy.swarm_dockers` (no migration): `GET /v1/organizations/{org}/deploy/destinations` (Index: the destinations of the usable servers — `Server::isUsable()`: reachable, usable, not a Swarm worker, not disabled, role deployment or both — per server by name, standalone before Swarm, plus what New Destination needs), `GET`/`PATCH`/`DELETE …/destinations/{uuid}` (Show, `Show::submit`, `Show::delete`), `GET …/destinations/{uuid}/resources` (Resources), and under a server `GET`/`POST …/servers/{uuid}/destinations` (Server/Destinations, `New/Docker::submit`) and `POST …/servers/{uuid}/destinations/scan`; every call inside `withOrg` with policy `deploy.resource.read`/`write`, a denial 404, reads carrying `canUpdate`/`canCreate`. Rules and messages: `name` required and a string, `network` required, a string, at most 255 characters and `DOCKER_NETWORK_PATTERN`, with Laravel's default messages; "Network already added to this server." (per server and kind, as Coolify checks), "Destination saved.", "You must delete all resources before deleting this destination.". The resources list and `attachedTo()` are real queries over the eleven tables that carry `destination_id`/`destination_type` (applications, services, nine database kinds; `destination_type` is Coolify's morph class, soft-deleted rows left out) joined to environment and project; nothing can be deployed yet, so the page shows its empty state. **Default destination:** creating a server now adds what Coolify's `created` hook adds (DP-37 had skipped it): a standalone destination with a new public id on network `coolify`, a row only, as Coolify saves it quietly. The network name is Coolify's byte for byte, because it is the Docker network on the server that the proxy and the generated commands name (design §1.2); the destination's name, a console label never written to a server, is `freeploy` where Coolify's is `coolify`. The hook's Swarm branch (`coolify-overlay`) cannot run for a new server and is not ported. Deleting a server now deletes its destinations too (the `forceDeleting` hook). Pages: `/destinations` (search, table and grid views, New destination), `/destination/{uuid}`, `/destination/{uuid}/danger`, `/destination/{uuid}/resources` and `/server/{uuid}/destinations`; the server menu's Destinations item (Networking, after Cloudflare Tunnel, disabled until the server is validated) is now a ported page. What needs the SSH executor, each following Coolify's own order of row and command: **adding** stores the row and reports "Creating the Docker network on the server needs the SSH executor, which is not available yet." on the destination's page (Coolify stores the row, then its `created` hook creates the network and ignores a failure; starting the proxy creates every destination's network anyway); a Swarm destination has no hook and is simply stored. **Deleting** a standalone destination answers 501 "Removing the Docker network from the server needs the SSH executor, which is not available yet." after the resources guard and keeps the row (Coolify removes the network first and deletes the row only when that worked), so a standalone destination cannot be deleted until W3, except with its server; a Swarm destination has no command and is deleted. **Scan networks** answers 501 and stores nothing, so the Discovered networks card and its "Add destination" (`Destinations::add`) never show and `add` has no route. Other departures: with validation unavailable no server is usable, so `/destinations` is its empty state without the New destination button and `/server/{uuid}/destinations` shows "Server validation required" (Coolify's rules); a destination is then reached by URL, and the API still lists a server's destinations and accepts `POST` for an unvalidated server, as `New/Docker::submit` does; a name over 255 characters is a validation error in Laravel's words (Coolify has no rule and shows the database error); the network pattern does not accept a trailing newline (PCRE's `$` does; Livewire trims it first); New Destination on a server's page offers that server only (Coolify also lists the other usable servers); the server is named by uuid in the path, not by numeric `serverId`; `Show::delete` and the API do not refuse the default network, only the page disables its button, as in Coolify; a Swarm destination's Resources page redirects from the browser, the API answering an empty list; the destination menu shows in the page rather than inside the main sidebar, with no Back entry (as the server menu, DP-37); the Swarm notice is DP-42's wording; audit log entries (`ui.destination.*`), the `name-changed` event and the command palette's settings search are not ported; the server list still counts 0 resources (`definedResources()` also needs additional destinations and services by server; nothing can be deployed yet); "Coolify" in the copy reads "Freeploy", and the view choice keeps Coolify's localStorage key.
- **Cloud tokens and cloud-init scripts (DP-45).** Coolify's Keys & Tokens → Cloud Tokens and Cloud-Init Scripts, and a server's Cloud Token page, in deploy-core-worker: `/v1/organizations/{org}/deploy/cloud-tokens` (list, add, show, rename, delete, `POST …/{uuid}/validate`), `/deploy/cloud-init-scripts` (list newest first, create, show, update, delete) and `GET`/`PATCH /deploy/servers/{uuid}/cloud-provider-token` with `POST …/validate`; every query inside `withOrg`. `CloudProviderTokenForm`'s and both `Show` components' rules with their messages and Laravel's defaults, and the components' toasts word for word. A provider is asked as Coolify asks it — one GET with the token as Bearer, ten seconds, valid when 2xx — and each of Coolify's three variants is kept as it is (the add form: Hetzner `/v1/servers`, DigitalOcean and Vultr `/v2/account`, Hostinger `/api/vps/v1/virtual-machines`; a stored token: Hetzner with `?per_page=1`; the server page: `validationEndpoint()`, then the server's own Hetzner server, Vultr instance or Hostinger VM, with Coolify's "cannot access this …" messages); the request goes through an injectable function, so no test touches the network. A token is stored only after its provider accepts it ("Invalid API token. Please check your token and try again." otherwise), encrypted with the per-org cipher (fp1, bound to `cloud_provider_tokens.token`) and never returned; a script is encrypted the same way (`cloud_init_scripts.script`). A token used by servers is not deleted ("… currently used by N server(s) …", 409). Uuids are Coolify's 24-character public ids. Pages: `/security/cloud-tokens`, `/security/cloud-tokens/{uuid}`, `/security/cloud-init-scripts`, `/security/cloud-init-scripts/{uuid}` and `/server/{uuid}/cloud-provider-token` (its menu item where Coolify's sidebar has it, shown when the server has a provider id), hand-ported from the Blade views; the sidebar already marked both Keys & Tokens children (DP-41). Departures: Coolify's two policies give every ability, listing included, to team admins only; here the lists and pages (never a secret) need `deploy.resource.read`, and every change, asking a provider with a stored token, and a script's content need `deploy.resource.write` (a reader gets `script: null`), and the sidebar shows both entries to every member; no cloud server can exist yet (provisioning on Hetzner, DigitalOcean, Vultr and Hostinger is not ported), so no server has a provider id, the Cloud Token menu item stays hidden and the page, reached by URL, shows "No cloud provider integration" — its set and validate paths are covered by tests only; `deploy.servers` has no `digitalocean_droplet_id` (Coolify's later migration is not in the folded schema), so a server is never taken for a DigitalOcean one and the droplet access check is left out, while DigitalOcean tokens are stored and validated like the others; `ValidCloudInitYaml` parses with symfony/yaml and the Worker has no YAML parser: a script starting with `#!` passes as in Coolify, and of Symfony's parse errors only a first content line indented with a tab is reproduced (with Coolify's two messages), so other invalid YAML is accepted; the provider is asked outside the database transaction, and where Coolify shows an exception's text ("Failed to validate token: …") it is the runtime's fetch error, not Guzzle's; a stored token of a provider Coolify does not know answers "Unknown provider." (the list's wording; its `Show` would say the Hetzner check failed); validating answers 200 with `{ valid, message }` and the page shows it as Coolify's success or error toast; the API names tokens by uuid (`cloud_provider_token_uuid`), not numeric id; Coolify's `auditLog()` events (`ui.cloud_token.*`, `ui.cloud_init_script.*`, `ui.server.cloud_token_*`) are not written, and its backfill of scripts without a uuid is not needed; each list has one editor dialog instead of one per row; a token or script page that is not found goes back to its list instead of a 404 page; for a member who cannot write the fields, Validate and Save are disabled rather than the page hidden; "Created" is UTC and "Updated … ago" is computed in the browser; "Coolify" in the titles reads "Freeploy".
- **Docker registries (DP-47).** Coolify's Integrations → Registries and a server's Registries page. **No table, nothing stored:** Coolify at 91b7e3e has no registry model or migration — a login lives only in the Docker config on the server (`~/.docker/config.json`, "Coolify does not save the token"), and each card reads that file over SSH — so there is no `encrypted` column to keep and Freeploy stores nothing either (no migration, no cipher). What the database does know is ported: `packages/db/src/deploy/registries.ts` lists the org's servers and, per server, `DockerRegistryLogins::usersOf()` as real queries in Coolify's order (applications with a registry image on the server's destinations, those using it as an additional server, the nine database kinds, each service's applications then databases, soft-deleted rows left out) and `applicationsBuiltOnBuildServers()` (by name: deployed to another server, a build pack that supports build servers, and that server deployments-only or the application's build server setting on). In deploy-core-worker: `GET /v1/organizations/{org}/deploy/registries` (a card per server by name, and the login form's providers), `POST …/registries` (multi-server login), `GET`/`POST …/servers/{uuid}/registries`, `POST …/servers/{uuid}/registries/{registry}/check` and `DELETE …/servers/{uuid}/registries/{registry}` (the servers route now takes four segments); every query inside `withOrg`. `normalizeRegistry`, `registryFromImage`, `REGISTRY_PATTERN`, `parseConfig` (names and usernames only; PHP's strict base64 and UTF-8 check reproduced), `rows()`, the seven `PROVIDERS` with their host patterns and `providerFor` are TypeScript; `Login::login`'s rules with its messages and Laravel's defaults word for word ("Choose at least one server.", "Enter a registry host, for example ghcr.io or registry.example.com:5000.", "Enter a valid {provider} host, for example …. Choose Other for a different registry.", "The username must not contain spaces."), `targetServers()` before the form (a chosen server that is not the org's: 403). A card of a server that is not functional reads Coolify's "The server is not reachable. Validate the server to read its registry logins." with the registries its resources use. What needs the SSH executor — all of it only commands, so each answers and stores nothing: **reading the logins** — a functional server's card says "Reading the Docker configuration on the server needs the SSH executor, which is not available yet." in its error line, so every row reads "Unknown" as Coolify shows it when the config cannot be read, never "Not logged in" (the reader is an injectable function, null in the Worker; with one, tests cover the logged-in rows and Coolify's "Could not read the Docker configuration on this server."); **docker login** (both forms) answers 501 "Running docker login on the server needs the SSH executor, which is not available yet." after validation, the password dropped; **the login check** answers the same 501; **docker logout** answers 501 "Running docker logout on the server needs the SSH executor, which is not available yet."; a registry name in a path that fails `REGISTRY_PATTERN` answers "The registry name is not valid." (422). Pages: `/registries` and `/server/{uuid}/registries`, hand-ported from the Blade views, the server menu's Registries item now a ported page (Operations, after GitHub Runners, shown to who may update, disabled until the server is validated); the sidebar already marked Integrations → Registries (DP-41). Departures: Coolify gives the overview to team admins and a server's registries to who may update the server, reading included; here every route, the reads too, needs `deploy.resource.write` (a denial 404; the overview then stays empty with an error toast, not Coolify's 403 page), while the sidebar shows the Registries entry to every member; no server can be validated yet, so every card shows the not-reachable line, New and the rows' Log in (functional servers only) never show, the multi-server form's server list is its "No reachable server to log in to" state, and the server menu item is disabled — the page is reached by URL; the actions menu of a row logged in through `auths` (Test, Edit and Delete in `x-table.row-actions`, the edit dialog's "Update login", the delete confirmation) is not ported, as no row can read as logged in before W3 — such a row shows the Test button of a credential helper's row, and `editRegistry` is honoured by the API only; the commands themselves (`docker login` with the secret on stdin, the check, `docker logout`, the sudo form, the error-line filter of `run()`), `registryAuthConfig`/`singleRegistryConfig` (the image scanner's) and the audit log entries (`ui.server.registry_*`) are left for the executor's task; Coolify's public API under `/api/v1/servers/{uuid}/registries` (its abilities and 400 answers) is not ported, only its route shape is followed; the login check on an invalid registry name answers 422 where the component silently does nothing; the cards come in one answer rather than lazily (no skeleton placeholder), and Refresh reads them all again; a resource's link is a path, not an absolute URL, and Coolify's per-project access grants (`ScopedByAccessGrants`) do not narrow the list; a username ending in a newline is refused (PCRE's `$` accepts it); registry names sort by code unit (PHP sorts numeric-looking names as numbers); the multiple listbox was added to `Listbox` (`values`), without Coolify's count label; "Coolify" in the copy reads "Freeploy".
- **CA certificate (DP-48).** Coolify's server CA Certificate page without SSH, over `deploy.ssl_certificates` (no migration): `GET /v1/organizations/{org}/deploy/servers/{uuid}/ca-certificate` (`Show::mount` + `loadCaCertificate`: the PEM certificate and its `valid_until`, both empty for a server that has none), `PATCH` (`saveCaCertificate { certificateContent }`) and `POST …/ca-certificate/regenerate` (`regenerateCaCertificate`); every query inside `withOrg`. Coolify's policy is followed: the page and the certificate are for every member (`mount()` authorizes nothing and the view's toggle is `@can('view')`), so `GET` needs `deploy.resource.read` and carries `canUpdate`; saving and regenerating are `manageCaCertificate` (team admin), so `deploy.resource.write`; a denial is 404. `ssl_certificate` and `ssl_private_key` are stored with the per-org cipher (fp1, each bound to `ssl_certificates.<column>`); the private key is in no answer. **The certificates** (`deploy-core-worker/src/ssl/`, Web Crypto only, no new dependency, on the DER helpers of DP-33's `ssh-keys.ts`, which now exports them): `generateSslCertificate` is `SslHelper::generateSslCertificate` for a CA and for a leaf (kept for the database pages) — EC secp521r1 for a CA and prime256v1 for a leaf; subject `CN, O=Coolify, C=XX, ST=Default` in `openssl_csr_new`'s order and string types (UTF8String, the country PrintableString); issuer the CA certificate's subject, or itself; a positive 63-bit random serial; `notBefore` now and `notAfter` `validityDays` × 86400 s later (UTCTime before 2050), which is also `valid_until`; the `[ v3_req ]` extensions in its order and criticality (CA: `basicConstraints` critical `CA:TRUE, pathlen:0`, `keyUsage` critical `keyCertSign, cRLSign`, `subjectKeyIdentifier` hash; leaf: `basicConstraints` critical `CA:FALSE`, `keyUsage` critical `digitalSignature, keyAgreement`, `extendedKeyUsage` `serverAuth, clientAuth`, `subjectKeyIdentifier`, `subjectAltName` with `DNS:<common name>` first, then the given names and the server's address as `IP:` or `DNS:`, each once); no `authorityKeyIdentifier`, because Coolify's section has none (so, as with Coolify's own, `openssl verify -x509_strict` refuses a leaf); signature ecdsa-with-SHA512 by the CA key or the certificate's own; a signing key that is not the CA certificate's is refused as `openssl_csr_sign` refuses it; PEM with 64-character lines, the key as PHP's `openssl_pkey_export` writes it on OpenSSL 3 (PKCS#8 `PRIVATE KEY`, the inner ECPrivateKey with its public key and without parameters — not SEC1 `EC PRIVATE KEY`). `x509.ts` is what `saveCaCertificate` asks of PHP: `openssl_x509_read` (the first certificate block as OpenSSL's PEM reader finds it: text and other blocks before it skipped, CRLF fine, a `BEGIN` line that does not start its line refused), `openssl_x509_export` (normalized PEM) and `openssl_x509_check_private_key` (EC, RSA and Ed25519 keys). Messages word for word: "Certificate content cannot be empty.", "Invalid certificate format.", "Failed to process certificate.", "This certificate does not match the CA private key of this server. Freeploy signs database certificates with that key, so clients would fail with a certificate signature error.", "CA Certificate saved successfully.", "CA Certificate regenerated successfully.". Tests: fixtures made by Coolify's own PHP code (PHP 8.3, OpenSSL 3.0) — the names and every extension but the key identifier of a generated CA and leaf are byte-identical to PHP's, and PHP's answers to read, export and key check are reproduced — and, when the binary is there, the system `openssl` parses the certificates, writes the same PEM back for certificate and key, verifies a leaf against its CA (also one signed with PHP's CA) and makes the custom certificates the key check is tried with. Page: `/server/{uuid}/ca-certificate` hand-ported from the Blade view (the validity badge, the callout, the read-only bind mount with its copy button, show/hide, the two confirmations that ask for the certificate path); the menu item was already where Coolify's sidebar has it (Settings, after Cloud Token) and is now a ported page. What needs the SSH executor, in Coolify's order of row and command: both actions store first and then write the file on the server (`writeCertificateToServer`) and queue `RegenerateSslCertJob` (every database certificate of the server signed again with the CA); here the change is stored and the answer carries Coolify's success message plus `unavailable` "Writing the CA certificate to the server needs the SSH executor, which is not available yet." (Coolify would show the SSH error instead of the success toast, with the row already changed); the job is not ported, and no database certificate can exist yet. `SslHelper::caCertificateFileCommands` is ported as a pure function with its exact output tested (`/data/coolify/ssl/coolify-ca.crt`, rewritten in place), for the executor to run. **A server without a CA certificate:** Coolify makes it while installing Docker (`InstallDocker`, part of validation) or when a database first needs it (`Server::ensureCaCertificate`), not when the server is created; neither point exists in Freeploy, so `createServer` is unchanged and a server has none until Regenerate — the page then shows no badge and an empty certificate, and saving a pasted certificate answers Coolify's success toast and stores nothing, exactly as Coolify's component does when there is no row (the answer says `stored: false`). Other departures: the paths in the copy stay `/data/coolify/ssl/coolify-ca.crt` and the certificate's organization and CA common name stay `Coolify` / `Coolify CA Certificate` (they are on the server and in the containers, design §1.2), while the mismatch message and the title read "Freeploy"; the link to the SSL guide still points at Coolify's documentation; saving keeps `valid_until` as generated even when the pasted certificate ends at another time (Coolify's behaviour, so the badge can be wrong for a custom certificate); the confirmations ask for the path but not the account password (deploy-core cannot check it yet), so their last button reads "Confirm" as Coolify's does when the password step is skipped; `auditLog()` (`ui.server.ca_certificate.updated` / `.regenerated`) is not written; PHP reads a `file://` path given as the certificate and this never does; only DER is read (no BER), the PEM reader is not limited to OpenSSL's line length, and OpenSSL's config-file syntax applied to the common name and alternative names (`$`, `#`, quotes) and the `otherName`, `dirName` and `RID` alternative names are not reproduced (an unknown type fails the generation); a CA key that is not EC cannot sign (Coolify only ever generates EC); a generation failure answers 500 with Coolify's "SSL Certificate generation failed: …"; the file storages `generateSslCertificate` writes for a database (`server.crt`, `server.key`, `server.pem`) and its delete-and-create are left to the callers (the CA's is in the handler); the API tests call the handler with an in-memory store and `route()` for the routing, since `route()` takes no store; the menu keeps Coolify's rule that CA Certificate is disabled until the server is validated, so with validation unavailable the page is reached by URL.
- **Fixtures.** `.gitignore` excludes `*.pem`, so DP-33's PEM key fixtures had stayed out of the repository; they are added explicitly (they are throwaway test keys).

- **Stale policy-worker (DP-38).** On stage every deploy-core route answered 404 to the org owner: the policy-worker was last deployed with #9, and #18 added `deploy.resource.read`/`write` to `packages/policy-engine` only. `orun plan --changed` planned `policy-engine` but not the policy-worker that declares `dependsOn` on it, so the Worker kept its old copy and denied the new actions. Until DP-38 nothing past the edge's 401 had been checked on stage for the deploy API. The same gap leaves billing-, integrations-, metering- and projects-worker on older copies of `packages/db`; Freeploy does not call them yet. Reported as a platform gap. Redeploying it did not by itself make the owner's requests pass, so DP-39 makes a denial outside prod say which step denied (membership's status or policy's reason; prod still answers the bare 404). Stage then said `unknown_action`: #18 granted `deploy.resource.read`/`write` to the roles but never added them to the engine's known actions, so they were denied before any role was consulted. DP-40 adds them (and redeploys the policy-worker, which the plan would not do for a package change alone); until then no signed-in request had reached the deploy API.

- **Stage parity after DP-41 (local run).** `/security/private-key`, `/servers` and `/profile` now differ from the reference only in fixture data: the reference's Parity User team owns two keys (`localhost`, `parity-key`) and the validated `localhost` server (4 resources, Ready), so its key table, server row and the profile's "Before you can delete your account" blocker appear, while the stage user owns nothing. A matching stage fixture needs a validated server, which needs the test VMs and the SSH executor (W1, W3); until then these rows are listed here, not masked.
- **One cron validator (DP-68).** Coolify's `validate_cron_expression()` (bootstrap/helpers/shared.php) is one function for every caller — Advanced's disk usage check, Docker Cleanup, scheduled tasks, backup schedules — and uses one library. Freeploy had two ports: DP-42's in `servers/settings-validation.ts` (also used by DP-58's scheduled tasks) re-implemented dragonmantank/cron-expression's field rules and accepted expressions Coolify refuses (weekday names in a range such as `MON-FRI`, `L`, `W`, `#`, `?`, `@midnight`, impossible dates); DP-65's clean-room `helpers/cron.ts` matches Coolify's verdicts. `validateCronExpression` now delegates to `helpers/cron.ts`, and Coolify's schedule words (`VALID_CRON_STRINGS`, `translate_cron_expression`) live in one module, `helpers/cron-presets.ts`, that both import. Verified: the shared validator gives Coolify's verdict for all 48 cron goldens (fixtures/s3, emitted by Coolify's real PHP), and the expressions the old tests used were checked against the real library too; the Advanced, Docker Cleanup and scheduled task routes now refuse `MON-FRI` and `@midnight` with Coolify's messages. DP-42's test asserted the old, wrong verdicts (`0 0 1 JAN MON-FRI`, `15,45 1-5/2 L * 5L` and `@midnight` valid); it now asserts Coolify's. A preset is matched as an own key only (the old `in` check also accepted names such as `toString`). Not ported: dragonmantank/cron-expression itself, which Coolify uses only to compute run times (Docker Cleanup's stalled-cleanup threshold, the scheduler) — that comes with the scheduler.
- **Proxy, security, Cloudflare Tunnel, Sentinel logs, transfer, resources and images (DP-96).** The server pages deploy-web still lacked, hand-ported from their Blade views (Coolify's copy, "Coolify" reading "Freeploy" where it names the product): `/server/{uuid}/proxy`, `/proxy/dynamic`, `/proxy/certificates`, `/proxy/logs`, `/security`, `/security/patches`, `/cloudflare-tunnel`, `/sentinel/logs`, `/transfer`, `/resources` and `/images` (`ServerProxy.tsx`, `ServerSecurity.tsx`, `ServerCloudflareTunnel.tsx`, `ServerSentinelLogs.tsx`, `ServerTransfer.tsx`, `ServerResources.tsx`, `ServerImages.tsx`; the browser's models in `src/lib/server-pages.ts`). The server menu (in the page, DP-37) now has `<x-server.sidebar>`'s children below the active item: Security → General, Patching and Vulnerabilities (the last two for a functional server), Proxy → Configuration, Dynamic Configurations and Logs (`proxySet()`) and TLS Certificates (Traefik), Sentinel → Configuration and Logs; a disabled item shows none, as Coolify's. In deploy-core-worker, `handlers/server-pages.ts`, routed by `handleServers`, answers `GET …/servers/{uuid}/{proxy,security,security/patches,cloudflare-tunnel,sentinel/logs,transfer,resources,images}` and the pages' actions, inside `withOrg` with `deploy.resource.read`/`write` and a 404 on denial (Coolify's view for the proxy, Cloudflare Tunnel, resources and images pages; update, viewSecurity, viewSentinel and transfer — admins, the owner for transfer — as write for the others, the reads too, as `can.update.resource` and the components' mount() do). It reuses DP-88's helpers over the server and server_settings rows rather than repeating them: `findServer`, `functional`, `proxyOf`, `saveSettings`, `setTunnel` (the updated hook's host key, the previous IP), `transferredAway`, `sentinelEnabled`, `sshFingerprints`, `definedResources` with the status accessors (`resourceStatus`, split out of `serverResourcesPayload`), and ServerTransferMigrator's own checks, moved out of `/api/v1`'s migrate into `transferStartError()` so both ask the same; `safeExternalUrlMessage` now takes anything with the console URL.
