No query, migration, binding or dependency is added. **Rows only, done:** TerminalAccess::toggleTerminal ("Terminal access has been enabled." / "…disabled."); CloudflareTunnel::manualCloudflareConfig ("Cloudflare Tunnel enabled.", `assertNotTransferredAway()`'s "This server is managed by another Coolify instance.") and toggleCloudflareTunnels for a tunnel set up by hand — Coolify runs `docker rm -f coolify-cloudflared` without waiting for it, so the setting is saved and the previous IP restored with Coolify's success or its "Action required" warning, and the answer adds that removing cloudflared needs the SSH executor; Security's host key (fingerprints, source, trust time, mode, the pending key's SHA-256 for Trust) and Patching's stored check; the transfer's metadata (the export id, the owner marker conflict, the migration's status, log, checklist and warnings, this instance's URL as DEPLOY_CONSOLE_URL); Resources' managed tab (`definedResources()`: the primary and additional applications, the nine database kinds, the services, with project, environment, `$resource->type()`, the status accessor and the resource's link), with Coolify's search, the five column sorts (natural, case-insensitive, stable after the name order) and pages of 10 (1–100). **What runs on the server answers 501** "… needs the SSH executor, which is not available yet." after Coolify's own checks and before anything is stored: reading the dynamic configurations and Traefik's acme.json; the proxy's and Sentinel's logs of a functional server (GetLogs reads nothing from one that is not: an empty log, as Coolify's viewer shows it); the four host key changes — Coolify's checks first (`canBeValidated()`'s "This server was transferred to another Coolify instance.", "There is no new host key to trust.", "The server sent another new host key. Reload the page and compare the key again.", "The host key check is disabled for this server. Reload the page and enable the check first.", a mode that is not warn or enforce changing nothing), then ServerConnectionCheckJob's connection, as `/api/v1`'s reset_host_key answers it (DP-88); Check for updates (after `ensureManagedHere()`); updating cloudflared and tearing down a tunnel Coolify manages through the Cloudflare API; Transfer server after the migrator's checks (https, SafeWebhookUrl with the console's allowed-targets link, the token, a transfer already running); the Unmanaged tab and the images of a functional server (none from one that is not, as `loadUnmanagedContainers()` and `load()` give). **Departures:** with no validated server, every page shows Coolify's unvalidated state (the proxy pages' "Server validation required", Patching's "Freeploy cannot reach {name}.", Cloudflare Tunnel's "Validate the server for automated setup" with its manual link), and the menu keeps Coolify's rule that Proxy, Sentinel, Resources, Images and Transfer are disabled until validation, so those pages are reached by URL; the functional branches are thin — the proxy configuration (Server\Proxy: the configuration file, routing behaviour, the custom error page, switching the proxy) says it needs the executor instead of its form, Dynamic Configurations has Reload but no "+ Add" (NewDynamicConfiguration writes the file over SSH), TLS Certificates its Refresh only, Patching its overview and Check for updates without the package table, frequency, automatic updates, Remove unused packages or Reboot (all run on the server; the hourly limit of the frequency needs the cron's run dates, not ported), the images list and Delete are not ported (nothing can be listed), nor the unmanaged containers' start, stop and restart; Cloudflare Tunnel's automated setups (an integration token through the Cloudflare API — Integration tokens are not ported — or a tunnel token with ConfigureCloudflared) are not ported, a validated server's page saying so; Transfer back is not ported (no server is ever transferred away from Freeploy: completing a transfer was cut in DP-88), the transferred-away branch keeps Coolify's paragraph without its form; the confirmations ask for the server name or Coolify's confirmation text, never the account password (deploy-core cannot check it yet); the host key helper drops Coolify's SSH_HOST_KEY_CHECKING paragraph and the instance mode is Coolify's default, warn (Freeploy reads no such variable); the Coolify host (id 0) does not exist in Freeploy, so Transfer's localhost branch never shows; Resources has no canvas toggle (server.canvas is not ported) and no image scan markers (no scan runs); the logs panel is the viewer's frame, empty state and Refresh, not GetLogs' search, follow, streaming and download; Coolify's polling (the connection check, a running transfer, a running patch check) and the audit log lines (`ui.server.terminal_access.*`, `ui.server.cloudflare_tunnel.*`, `ui.server.migrate`) are not ported; the console messages repeat DP-88's texts as literals because the handler loads inside the router's import cycle. **Cut:** GitHub Runners — not row-only: its save asks the GitHub API for the runner group (GithubRunnerApi::ensureRunnerGroup) and checks Sysbox over SSH, and its executions come from runners Freeploy cannot start; `/security/vulnerabilities` (image scanning) was not in the task, and the old `/security/image-scanning`, `terminal-access` and `host-key` redirects with it. Tests: `apps/deploy-web/test/routes.test.ts` (the eleven pages, the paths left not ported) and `test/server-pages.test.ts` (Str::headline(), the resource list, the badges); `tests/deploy-core-worker/src/server-pages.test.ts` over the API fixture's rows in PGlite (row-level security on): each route's authorization, another org's server never found, the messages, what is stored and that each 501 stores nothing; `servers.test.ts` now takes `/github-runners` as its unknown path. Not verified: stage and the visual gate.

## Notes on DP5

- **Projects and environments (DP-46).** Coolify's Projects without resources: `/v1/organizations/{org}/deploy/projects` in deploy-core-worker (list with the environment and resource counts, add, show with its environments, rename, delete) and `…/projects/{uuid}/environments` (list, add, show, rename, delete), every call inside `withOrg` with policy `deploy.resource.read`/`write`; reads carry `canWrite`, or `canUpdate` and `canDelete`, for the views' `@can`. `ValidationPatterns::nameRules()` and `descriptionRules()` with their messages word for word, shared with the private key forms. A new project gets its `deploy.project_settings` row and a "production" environment as `Project`'s created hook does, each row with the org; an environment name is stored as `Environment::customizeName()` stores it (lower case, trimmed, `/` → `-`); environments list in `Environment::compareNames` order (production, staging, development, then natural order). A project or environment with resources is not deleted: `projectHasResources` / `environmentHasResources` count the rows that are not soft-deleted in `applications`, `services` and the nine `standalone_*` tables, and the answer is 409 with the Livewire components' messages ("Project {name} has resources defined, please delete them first.", "Environment {name} has defined resources, please delete them first."). Uuids are Coolify's 24-character public ids. Pages, hand-ported from the Blade views: `/projects` (grid and table, search, sort, paging, New Project), `/project/{uuid}` (the environments, New Environment), `/project/{uuid}/edit`, `/project/{uuid}/environment/{env_uuid}` (the header and "No resources yet" only) and `/project/{uuid}/environment/{env_uuid}/edit`; the layout marks Projects current on `projects`, `projects/*` and `project/*` as navbar.blade.php does (also on project routes not ported yet). The list toolbar parts (`<x-table.dropdown>`, `<x-table.sort-header>`, `<x-client-pagination>`, `<x-page-size-select>`, `<x-canvas-view-toggle>`) are `src/components/CoolifyList.tsx`. **Not checked in a browser or on stage yet**: the pages are type-checked, linted and built, and the API is covered by tests; the visual and DOM gates have not seen them. Departures: `GET …/deploy/projects` now answers `{ projects, canWrite }` (it answered a bare array of uuid, name and description; nothing called it); authorization is the org-wide `deploy.resource.read`/`write`, without Coolify's per-project access grants (`AccessResolver`: members limited to some projects, separate write and delete levels), so `canDelete` equals `canUpdate`; Coolify keeps environment names unique per project with a `unique(name, project_id)` index and reports a violation as "Duplicate entry found. Please use a different name."; the folded schema has no such index, so the service asks inside the transaction and answers 409 with that message (two requests at the same moment could both pass; no migration was added); deleting an environment or a project also deletes its shared variables, which Coolify's deleting hooks set out to do but, as the source reads, do not (they loop over the relation object, not its rows); the "has resources" messages drop Coolify's `<strong>` tags (the toast is plain text); the audit trail (`Auditable`) and the global search cache are not ported; the canvas views are not ported: the Canvas links stay and lead to the not-ported notice, and a page never redirects to a remembered canvas; the table or grid choice is remembered in the browser (`freeploy.view.*`) instead of a cookie per user, and the page size under `freeploy.page-size.*`; project icons are not ported (every project shows the default glyph, the edit page has no Icon block, `/project/{uuid}/icon` is not served), while the section's copy still says "Icon, name, and description"; the search filters on every keystroke (Coolify waits 150 ms); the top bar shows the project and environment switchers after the "Projects" link, which Coolify hides on these pages (the layout is generated with that link), and the switchers open without Coolify's fade; an edit page sends a member who cannot update back to the project or environment, where Coolify's route answers 403; the delete dialogs are the existing one-step port of `<x-modal-confirmation>` (the name is typed, no password); the environment page has no resource list (no resource type is ported; an environment that somehow has resources shows a "not available yet" notice), and its "New resource", "Add resource", "Shared variables" and "Clone environment" links, like "Open onboarding" on an empty `/projects`, lead to pages not ported yet; PHP's `strnatcasecmp` is re-implemented from strnatcmp.c; "Created" is the stored UTC date; `/api/v1/projects` (Coolify's own JSON API) is DP9; "Coolify" in the titles reads "Freeploy".
- **New resource and Docker image applications (DP-49).** Coolify's New Resource page, creating an application from a Docker image, and that application's General configuration, without SSH, over `deploy.applications` and `deploy.application_settings` (no migration): `GET /v1/organizations/{org}/deploy/projects/{uuid}/environments/{env_uuid}/new` (Select: the usable servers — `Server::isUsable()` — by name with their destinations, the usable build servers apart with `onlyBuildServerAvailable`, and Select's catalogue of five Git based and three Docker based application types and nine databases with name, description and logo as `loadServices()` defines them, each marked `creatable`: only `docker-image`), `POST …/deploy/applications` (`DockerImage::submit`, `type: "dockerimage"`), `GET`/`PATCH …/deploy/applications/{uuid}` (Configuration with General's mount data, `General::submit`), `POST …/applications/{uuid}/{deploy,restart,stop}` and `GET …/projects/{uuid}/environments/{env_uuid}/resources` (Resource/Index's list); every call inside `withOrg` with policy `deploy.resource.read`/`write`, a denial 404, reads carrying `canUpdate`/`canDeploy`/`canCreate`; the New Resource data needs `write` (Coolify's `createAnyResource`). **What is stored** for a new application, as `DockerImage::submit` and the model hooks do: a 24-character public id, `name` `docker-image-<uuid>`, `repository_project_id` 0, `git_repository` `coollabsio/coolify` and `git_branch` `main` (Coolify's placeholders, byte for byte), `build_pack` `dockerimage`, `ports_exposes` `80`, `health_check_enabled` false, the environment, the destination with Coolify's morph class, `compose_parsing_version` `5`, four `manual_webhook_secret_*` of 40 random characters each encrypted with the per-org cipher (fp1, bound to `applications.<column>`), `fqdn` from `generateUrl()` (the server's wildcard domain, else `http://<uuid>.<ip>.sslip.io`, an IPv6 address as sslip's dashed label) passed through the saving hook's `DomainPortOverrides::normalize`, every other column the schema's default (status `exited`), and one `application_settings` row of defaults; both rows carry the org. The image is parsed by a port of `DockerImageParser`: no tag is `latest`, a digest is stored as `<name>@sha256` with the tag `sha256-<hash>`, a registry port is not a tag. Nothing is deployed and no deployment is queued — Coolify's `submit` only redirects to the configuration page. Rules and messages: `ValidationPatterns`' Docker image name and tag patterns (the tag's `$VAR` / `${VAR:-default}` forms included), the SHA256 rule and "Provide either a tag or SHA256 digest, not both." with Laravel's default messages for the Livewire properties ("The image name field …"); General's rules for the fields of a Docker image application — name, description, `fqdn` (`applicationDomainRules` only when it changes, with PHP's `parse_url` and `FILTER_VALIDATE_URL` re-implemented for http(s) URLs), image name (required) and tag, ports exposes, port mappings, network aliases (the model's mutator: stored as a JSON list), custom Docker options (shell-safe, at most 2000 characters, trimmed), pre/post deployment commands and their container names — with General's `messages()` and `validationAttributes` word for word. A changed `fqdn` is normalized (`normalizeApplicationDomains`), stored without ports with the ports in `domain_port_overrides`, its `noindex_domains` synced, refused with 409 and the conflicts when another application of the org uses a domain (`checkDomainUsage`) unless `forceSaveDomains`, and answered with Coolify's sslip-with-https warning in place of "Application settings updated!". The environment and project "has resources" guards (DP-46) and a destination's `attachedTo()` and Resources page (DP-44) now see real rows (a test in `tests/db` proves it). Pages, hand-ported from the Blade views: `/project/{uuid}/environment/{env_uuid}/new` (Select's type step with search, list and grid views and the resource type filter, then the server and destination steps with Coolify's skipping of a step with one choice, ending on `?type=…&destination=…&server_id=…`; there the Docker Image form, which splits a pasted reference as `updatedImageName` does), `/project/{uuid}/environment/{env_uuid}/application/{app_uuid}` (the heading with the status summary and Deploy / Deploy (without cache) / Remove container, the configuration menu with every entry of `configuration-sidebar.blade.php` in its groups, and General: Application details, Access with the public access card and Internal access, Build & deploy with the image and the deployment commands, the Networking dialog, Container with the custom Docker options), and the environment page now lists its applications as a table or a grid with search, sort and paging. Coolify's resource logos (`public/svgs/resources/*`, `gitea.svg`) are copied to `apps/deploy-web/public/svgs`. **Not checked in a browser or on stage yet**: the pages are type-checked, linted and built, and the API is covered by tests. What needs the SSH executor and the deployment Workflow answers 501: "Deploying the application needs the SSH executor, which is not available yet.", and the same for Restarting and Stopping (Stop is also the exited application's "Remove container"); the page shows the answer as an error toast. There is no `DELETE` of an application (Coolify's delete runs commands on the server) and the Danger Zone page is not ported, so an application — and with it its environment, project and a standalone destination — cannot be removed until W3. Departures: with validation unavailable no server is usable, so on stage the server step shows "No available servers" and an application can only be created through the API, which — as `find_resource_destination_for_current_team()` — accepts any destination of the org on a server that is not build-only; the page's `server_id` is the server's uuid and the API's `server_uuid` is optional but, when given, must be the destination's server (Coolify reads only the destination); a destination that is missing answers 404 "Destination not found." (Coolify throws it as an exception); **container labels are not ported**: `generateLabelsApplication()` (Traefik and Caddy labels) is deferred, so `custom_labels` stays null where Coolify fills it on the first visit of General and on every save, `resetDefaultLabels` has no route, `customLabels` in a `PATCH` is ignored, and the Labels subsection (label management, special characters, Active labels) is not on the page — the deployment job will have to generate labels before the first deploy; **HTTP Basic Authentication** (it only takes effect through the generated labels) and the two container-label settings are not on General and not accepted by `PATCH`; fields of other build packs (build pack, Git repository and branch, commands, directories, Dockerfile, Compose, static site, Nginx, watch paths, redirect) are ignored by `PATCH`, which is partial: a field left out keeps its value, where Livewire always sends every property; a blank image name is refused before anything is saved (Coolify saves the other fields first and then reports it); Livewire's string trimming is not assumed (values are validated as sent; a trailing newline does not pass a pattern as PCRE's `$` lets it); domains are edited on the Domains page as in Coolify, which is not ported, so `fqdn` changes only through the API, without Coolify's DNS check (`validateDNSEntry`, a toast that does not stop the save), without managed DNS records, and with conflicts checked against applications only (no services yet; a Compose application's service domains are not read); `generateUrl()` does not resolve a server given by hostname to its IP (the Worker has no resolver; Coolify falls back to the name too when it does not resolve) and `host.docker.internal` has no instance IP to fall back on; a wildcard domain that does not parse as a URL falls back to sslip; the sslip warning drops its HTML; the catalogue's `logo` is a path under `/svgs` without Coolify's cache-busting query, its documentation links still point to coolify.io's docs, and the database website links drop `?ref=coolify.io`; one-click services, their categories (the category filter is shown disabled) and "Updated …" are empty until DP8 ports the 390 templates, so the Services section and its trademark notice do not show; the database image steps (PostgreSQL, Redis / Valkey), `existing-postgresql` and every type but Docker Image end on a "not available yet" notice after the server and destination steps; the types load with the page (no skeleton; Reload fetches again) and the search field has no debounce; Deploy canvas links to `/canvas`, not ported; the heading's actions stay in the page at every width (Coolify moves them to the top bar from xl) and the configuration menu stays in the page instead of the main sidebar, as the server menu does (DP-37); the status is not polled, and the star toggle, the links menu, the deploying indicator, the restart-limit warning, the image scan gate and the configuration checker are not ported; Stop's and Remove container's dialogs are the existing one-step port of `<x-modal-confirmation>` without the "cleanup" checkbox; the menu shows Terminal and Files to who may write (Coolify's `canAccessTerminal`), has no server status badge on Servers and no security dot, and its Git Source entry hides for a Docker image application as `git_based()` says; Internal access shows "No deployed container found" for the hostname (nothing runs) and plain read-only fields without copy buttons; helper texts are plain text (the docs link of the custom Docker options helper is dropped); a member who cannot update sees the General fields disabled and no commands or Docker options (Coolify's `canViewSecrets`); authorization is org-wide, without Coolify's per-project access grants or its separate deploy level (`canDeploy` equals `canUpdate`); the environment list has no filters (type, status, server, tags), no tags, no image scan marker and no favicon, shows only applications, remembers the view under `freeploy.view.environment` and the page size under `freeploy.page-size.environment-resources`, and `server_status` and the restart counters are computed but nothing can run yet; the audit trail, the `name-changed` event and the global search cache are not ported; `/api/v1/applications/dockerimage` (Coolify's own JSON API) is DP9; "Coolify" in the copy reads "Freeploy".
- **Shared variables (DP-50).** Coolify's Shared Variables for the four scopes — the org (Coolify's team), a project, an environment, a server — over `deploy.shared_environment_variables`. The plan lists shared variables under DP9; they are brought forward because application environment variables (DP5) resolve them. API in deploy-core-worker, every query inside `withOrg`: `GET /v1/organizations/{org}/deploy/shared-variables` (the overview), `GET …/projects`, `…/environments`, `…/servers` (the index pages), and for a scope `team`, `project/{uuid}`, `environment/{uuid}` or `server/{uuid}`: `GET …/{scope}` (the variables without values, and the developer view's text), `POST …/{scope}` (`saveKey`), `POST …/{scope}/bulk` (the developer view's `submit`), `GET`/`PATCH`/`DELETE …/{scope}/{id}` (`Show::loadValues`, `submit`, `delete`) and `POST …/{scope}/{id}/lock`. A variable has no uuid in Coolify, so `{id}` is its row id, looked up within the scope. `ValidationPatterns`' key rules and messages word for word (trimmed, `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_.]*`, at most 255), `Add`'s and `Show`'s rules with Laravel's defaults under each component's attribute names ("The multiline field is required." on add, "The is multiline field is required." on update), "Variable already exists.", "Cannot create predefined variable.", "Environment variable updated.", "Environment variable deleted successfully.", "Environment variables updated.", "The value cannot contain a NUL character.". `value` is stored with the per-org cipher (fp1, bound to `shared_environment_variables.value`). **Who sees a value** follows Coolify's policy on the two actions Freeploy has: `deploy.resource.read` gives the pages, keys, comments and flags (Coolify's `view`), `deploy.resource.write` gives values and every change (`viewSensitive`, `update`, `delete`: team admins for team and server variables, write access for a project's or environment's); a reader's developer view reads "KEY=(Hidden, only admins can view)" and the edit dialog "Hidden (only admins can view)"; a denial is 404. A locked variable (`is_shown_once`) is in no answer: the lock is permanent (the model's saving hook, here in the UPDATE itself), its value can be replaced (`newLockedValue`) but never read, and what the form sends as `value` is not stored. **Developer view:** `parseEnvFormatToArray`, `formatEnvValueForDevView`, `formatEnvVariableForDevView` and `parseEnvDevView` are TypeScript with PHP's `trim()` character list, PCRE's `\s` and a PHP array's key order reproduced, tested against Coolify's own cases (`ParseEnvFormatToArrayTest`, `EnvDevViewDirectiveTest`, `FormatEnvValueForDevViewTest`: quotes, inline comments, duplicates, placeholders, the seven directive errors); the save is `handleBulkSubmit` as written — parse first, delete the variables whose keys the text no longer has, then update or create per line in one transaction, leaving a locked or multiline variable and one the format cannot hold as they are; a key is matched as typed and stored trimmed (the key mutator), and only the server view writes a line's inline comment, so it clears the comment of a line without one (Coolify's behaviour, kept). The `# coolify:` directive is kept as Coolify writes it — it is the format's token, so a developer view copied from Coolify reads the same — and so are the parser's messages that quote it. **Predefined server variables:** creating a server now adds `COOLIFY_SERVER_UUID` and `COOLIFY_SERVER_NAME` (literal, the server's uuid and name) as `Server`'s created hook does, which DP-37 had deferred; the keys are Coolify's byte for byte because user compose files reference them (`{{server.COOLIFY_SERVER_UUID}}`). They are the read-only rows of the server's page, whose values every reader sees as in Coolify's view, left out of the developer view and the overview, never deleted or written by a developer view save, and (as in Coolify) not renamed when the server is; deleting a server deletes its variables (Coolify's foreign key). **Migration `590_deploy_shared_variables_server_scope`** (manifest entry with its checksum; the only migration of this task): Coolify widens the `type` check to `'server'` in a raw `DB::statement`, which the schema fold does not read, so `500_deploy_core` had the `server_id` column but refused the type; 590 is that statement. Coolify's `unique(key, project_id | environment_id | server_id, team_id)` indexes are not in the folded schema either; as for environment names (DP-46) the service asks before it writes. Pages, hand-ported from the Blade views: `/shared-variables` (the scope cards, Most used, Recently changed, Not used), `/shared-variables/team`, `/shared-variables/projects`, `/shared-variables/project/{uuid}`, `/shared-variables/environments`, `/shared-variables/environments/project/{project_uuid}/environment/{environment_uuid}`, `/shared-variables/servers` and `/shared-variables/server/{uuid}` — `<x-shared-variables.editor>` with its search, sort, "Add variable", the row's "Edit environment variable" dialog (lock, delete, value fetched when the dialog or the copy button asks) and the developer view with the unsaved bar; the sidebar's Shared Variables submenu already carried navbar.blade.php's patterns (DP-41) and is now tested for every page. Departures: a server created before this task has no predefined variables — Coolify backfills them in a migration, which cannot produce a value under the org's key, so a backfill is left for the task that first reads them; the overview counts no usage yet — which resources reference a variable comes from the resources' own environment variables, which nothing has yet, so "Most used" is empty, every variable lists under "Not used" and no reference is "missing"; renaming a variable to a key its scope already has answers 409 "Duplicate entry found. Please use a different name." in every scope, and so does a developer view line whose trimmed key exists (Coolify leaves both to the unique indexes, which do not cover team variables); a comment over 256 or a key over 255 characters in the developer view answers Laravel's message (Coolify lets the database refuse it); a predefined server variable answers 404 to a change, lock or delete (Coolify has no component for it); an empty value is stored as an empty string (the column is NOT NULL) and an empty comment as null; one org-level pair of actions stands for Coolify's per-project access levels (read, read:sensitive, write), which Freeploy does not model; the canvas views are not ported — the Canvas switch, a row's "Connect … to resources" link and the overview's rows keep Coolify's canvas URLs and lead to the not-ported notice, and a page never redirects to a remembered canvas; a scope that does not exist sends the visitor to its index page (Coolify: the dashboard, or 404 for an environment); the top bar has no project, environment or server switcher on these pages; project icons are not ported; the searches filter on every keystroke (Coolify waits 150 ms); the Add dialog stays open when the save fails (Coolify closes it at the click); Update is disabled until the value has loaded; the dialogs are the existing one-step port of `<x-modal-confirmation>`; "Coolify keeps the space." and "managed by Coolify" read "Freeploy". Deferred: resolving `{{team.KEY}}`, `{{project.KEY}}`, `{{environment.KEY}}` and `{{server.KEY}}` into a resource's environment at deploy time, with the "Resolved value" field and the `{{` autocomplete of a resource's variable forms, belongs to application environment variables and the deployment job; the model's `Auditable` trail (no audit log yet); a server transfer's handling of these variables; Coolify's own JSON API for shared variables (DP9).
- **Application domains, advanced, health check and resource limits (DP-52).** Four of an application's configuration pages for a Docker image application, without SSH, over `deploy.applications` and `deploy.application_settings` (no migration): `GET`/`PATCH /v1/organizations/{org}/deploy/applications/{uuid}/{domains,advanced,healthcheck,resource-limits}` in deploy-core-worker (`handlers/application-settings.ts`, dispatched from `handleApplications`), every call inside `withOrg` with policy `deploy.resource.read`/`write`, a denial 404, reads carrying the application for the frame and `canUpdate`/`canDeploy`. Everything these pages save only takes effect at the next deployment, so it is simply stored. **Resource Limits** (`ResourceLimits`): `PATCH` is `submit()` — its defaults first (an empty memory field is `0`, an empty swappiness 60, empty CPU shares 1024, an empty CPU set null), then `$rules` with `$messages` word for word ("Maximum Memory Limit must be a number followed by a unit (b, k, m, g). …"), CPU shares and swappiness stored as integers. **Healthcheck** (`HealthChecks`): `PATCH` is `submit()` / `instantSave()` (the same save, "Healthcheck updated.") with `rules()` and Laravel's default messages ("The health check path field …"), `isValidHealthCheckHost` ported, or `toggleHealthcheck()` with `{ toggle: true }` ("Healthcheck enabled." / "disabled.", or Coolify's restart notice as `info` when the application is running); the command and the expected response text are returned only to who can update (`canViewSecrets`); the page is 404 for a Docker Compose application as in `configuration.blade.php`. **Advanced** (`Advanced`): `PATCH` is `submit()` (the GPU form, with its checks: a count or device ids, not both; a count that is a number or `all`; options that parse), `instantSave()` with `{ instantSave: true }` (a listbox), or — named by `save` — `customName`, `customNamePrefix` (both saved as a slug, unique among the server's applications' uuids, prefixes and custom names, the prefix at most 30 characters), `certificateResolver` (the server's, `none`, or a custom name), `stopGracePeriod` (1–3600 or empty), `rollingUpdateDrainSeconds` (0–300), `maxRestartCount` (on `applications`), with the `#[Validate]` rules and the handlers' own rules, attributes and messages; a log drain switched on while the server has none is saved off with the rest and answered with "Log drain is not enabled on this server." (422), as `instantSave()` does; the read carries `isTraefikProxy`, the server's resolver, `labelsManaged` and the server's detected GPUs from `server_metadata`. **Domains** (`Domains`, for an application that is not a Compose one): `GET` answers the domains as rows (public URL, internal port with `has_port_override` and the inherited first exposed port, noindex flag, stored DNS status, the edit dialog's parts; a failed check first), the redirect, the two redirect switches, `labelsAreWritable`, the DNS target (`ServerDnsAddresses`: public IPv4, else public IPv6, else the server's address) and its label; `PATCH` carries `action`: `add` (`addDomain`: parts or a URL list through `applicationDomainRules`, lower-cased, **its www or non-www counterpart added with it** unless it is an IP, localhost, sslip.io or nip.io, "Domain … is already configured."), `update` (`updateDomain`: one URL, its port — an empty one removes the override —, search indexing, the www redirect with its missing counterparts added when it becomes `www` or `non-www`, the counterpart moved along after Regenerate hostname), `remove`, `generate` (`generateUrl()` for the application's uuid, then `syncRedirectDomainPairs`), `regenerateHostname` (answers a hostname, stores nothing), `redirect` (`setRedirect` with `ensureWwwNonWwwPairsConfigured` and "Redirect requires both www and non-www domains …"), `forceHttps`, `permanentRedirect` and `checkDns`. A save runs the model's saving hook as DP-49 ported it (ports to `domain_port_overrides`, a www counterpart inheriting its pair's port, noindex flags of gone domains dropped) and prunes `domain_dns_statuses` to the domains left; another application's domain answers 409 with the conflicts until `forceSaveDomains`, and a port that is neither exposed nor used by a domain (`portRequiresConfirmation`) answers 409 with `unrecognizedPort` until `forceUseUnknownPort`; the sslip-with-https warning comes back as `warning`; domains are refused while container labels are writable, with Coolify's two messages. **DNS checks are not available yet**: Coolify queues `CheckDomainDnsJob`, which asks DNS servers from the Coolify host; Freeploy has no such job, so a check is stored the way Coolify stores one whose job could not be queued (`markUrlsDnsCheckUnavailable`: `skipped`, "DNS check could not be started.", with `expected_ip`, `checked_at` and a `check_id`) and the answer carries `unavailable: "Checking DNS is not available yet."` in place of Coolify's "… could not be started. Try again …" toast; what `CheckDomainDns` decides without asking DNS is stored as it decides it — an sslip.io host is `ok` "DNS looks correct.", no server is `skipped` "No server available for DNS validation." — so a generated domain reads "DNS matches" as in Coolify. **Refresh GPUs** (`gatherServerMetadata` over SSH) answers 422 "Validate the server connection before you refresh GPUs." for a server that is not functional, else 501 "Refreshing GPUs needs the SSH executor, which is not available yet.". Pages, hand-ported from the Blade views inside `ApplicationWorkspace` (whose menu already linked to them): `/project/{uuid}/environment/{env_uuid}/application/{app_uuid}/domains` (the table with search, the DNS menu, Add domain with `<x-forms.domain-input>` and Generate domain, the row menu — Check DNS, Edit, Delete —, the Domain settings dialog, Redirects, the conflict and port dialogs), `/advanced`, `/healthcheck` and `/resource-limits`. **Not checked in a browser or on stage yet**: the pages are type-checked, linted and built, and the API is covered by tests. Departures: every `PATCH` is partial — a field left out keeps its stored value, where Livewire always sends every property (so a stored legacy value is not re-validated unless sent); the Domains component has no `submit()`, so its actions are one `PATCH` with `action`, and Advanced's self-saving fields one `PATCH` with `save` (the router allows two path segments below `/applications`); a refusal rolls the whole request back — in Coolify a redirect's or a generated domain's pairing can be saved before the conflict dialog shows, here nothing is stored until the save is forced, and `generate` takes `forceSaveDomains` for that; **container labels are still not generated** (DP-49), so no save here runs `resetDefaultLabels()` — force HTTPS, the redirect type, the www redirect, gzip, strip prefix, the resolver and the domains reach the proxy only once the deployment job generates labels; **managed DNS is not ported**: Cloudflare Domain Connect, DNS providers, the Manual records dialog, "Also delete the DNS record created by Coolify" on Delete, releasing or configuring records after an edit, and the DNS-mismatch gate of the add and edit dialogs (it only exists with a real check); the DNS statuses are pruned on every domain save (Coolify prunes on add, remove, generate and redirect) and nothing polls them; a failed DNS badge does not open the records dialog; `is_dns_validation_enabled` and `custom_dns_servers` are instance settings Freeploy does not have (validation reads enabled); the DNS target does not resolve a server given by hostname (the Worker has no resolver; Coolify shows the name too when it does not resolve); `checkDns` announces a finished check in the same answer (Coolify's poll does it 2 s later); conflicts are checked against the org's applications only (no services yet) and the conflict dialog names the resource without a link; row favicons (`DomainFavicon`), the suggested www rows (never built: the component does not call `buildSuggestedWwwRows`) and `toggleNoindexDomain` (not used by the view) are not ported; Docker Compose service domains and per-service redirects are not ported (no Compose application can be created yet); `updateDomain` and `removeDomain` answer 404 "Domain not found." for a URL the application does not have (Coolify returns silently). Healthcheck: `rules()` is taken as what `validate()` uses, on the reading that a component's `rules()` replace the same properties' `#[Validate]` attributes (not verified against Livewire's source, which the reference checkout does not vendor) — so the attribute's `required_if:healthCheckType,cmd` does not apply and a `cmd` check may be saved without a command (the view's `required` asks for one); the toggle saves the flag only (Coolify also writes the open form's unvalidated fields); blank port, command and response text are stored as null (Livewire stores the empty string); `customHealthcheckFound` is read, never written; the Enable dialog's guide link is dropped. Advanced: only what a Docker image application's view shows is read — the Git (auto deploy, queued and building deployments, submodules, LFS, clone depth) and Docker Compose (raw deployment, predefined network) sections, `isForceHttpsEnabled` (edited on Domains) and `isBuildServerEnabled` are ignored, and the folded schema has no `is_auto_cancel_*` columns; gzip, strip prefix and the resolver are accepted whatever the label mode and proxy (the component does not check; only the view hides them); `parseGpuOptions` reads a YAML mapping as flat `key: value` lines (no YAML parser in the Worker; a nested or flow value is refused as in Coolify) and `Str::slug` strips accents and drops what is still not ASCII (Laravel transliterates with portable-ascii); GPU count, device ids and options cleared in the form are stored as null; the "in use" checks see the server's applications of the org (a server belongs to one org) and skip when the application has no server; `instantSave()`'s Compose `parse()` is taken to do nothing for a Docker image application and is not run; `additional_servers` are not ported, so the GPU list shows the destination's server only; the audit trail (`auditLog`) is not ported; the GPU subsection's helper is a line of text with its link (the other HTML helpers are plain text). Resource Limits and all four: a validation error is a 422 with the messages by field (Resource Limits shows each as a toast, as Coolify does); numbers sent as JSON numbers are read as their text; PCRE's `$` accepting a trailing newline is not reproduced; the configuration-changed notice (`ConfigurationChecker`) is not ported — it compares with the last deployment and nothing deploys yet; the pages keep Configuration's title and have no section submenu; the edit dialog's listboxes are not portalled and the dialogs open without Coolify's transitions; authorization is org-wide (`canDeploy` equals `canUpdate`); "Coolify" in the copy reads "Freeploy".
- **Application environment variables (DP-51).** Coolify's Environment Variables page of an application, without SSH, over `deploy.environment_variables` (no migration; a variable belongs to its application through Coolify's `resourceable_type` `App\Models\Application` and `resourceable_id`): under `/v1/organizations/{org}/deploy/applications/{uuid}/environment-variables` — `GET` (All: the four switches, `showPreview`, the production then the preview variables with their flags and never a value, the shared variable keys the value field suggests, `canManage`, `canViewValues`), `POST` (`Add::submit` → `handleSingleSubmit`), `GET`/`PUT …/dev` (`getDevView`, and `submit`'s bulk save `{ variables, variablesPreview }`), `PATCH …/settings` (`instantSave`, `saveCoolifyDefaultVariables`, `saveLogRedactionSetting` on `application_settings`), `GET`/`PATCH`/`DELETE …/{env_uuid}` (`Show::loadValues` with `copyValue`, `submit`, `delete`) and `POST …/{env_uuid}/lock`; routed from `handleApplications` (the applications path now takes up to four segments) to its own handler, every call inside `withOrg` with policy `deploy.resource.read`/`write`, a denial 404. **What is stored** for a new variable, as the model's hooks do: a 24-character public id, the key normalized (`Str::trim`) and matching `ENVIRONMENT_VARIABLE_KEY_PATTERN`, the value without its edge line breaks unless it has a line break inside (`withoutEdgeLineBreaks`) and encrypted with the per-org cipher (fp1, bound to `environment_variables.value`) — a null value as NULL, an empty one as a ciphertext, which is what Coolify's "required and no stored value" order tells apart —, `is_shared` when the whole value is one `{{team|project|environment|server.KEY}}` reference, `version` `4.7.6` (Coolify's `constants.coolify.version` at 91b7e3e), the order after the last production variable, the org; a production variable gets its preview copy without an order when the key has none (the created hook), also on an application that shows no previews; a second row of a key is refused under an advisory lock as `performInsert` does. A lock (`is_shown_once`) is permanent: no route unlocks, a locked value is never returned (list, value, developer view, copy), and `newLockedValue` replaces it. `showPreview` is Coolify's rule (a Git repository and no Dockerfile of its own), so a Docker image application, which carries Coolify's placeholder repository, shows previews. The list comes in `managedEnvironmentVariablesQuery`'s order — required and empty by the resolved value, then `SERVICE_FQDN`/`SERVICE_URL`/`SERVICE_NAME` (SQL `LIKE`, its `_` any character), then `order` and id, or the key with the "Alphabetical" switch. **The developer view** is a port of `parseEnvFormatToArray`, `formatEnvValueForDevView`, `formatEnvVariableForDevView` and `parseEnvDevView` — it is not a dotenv parser: no escapes, `export` stays in the key and is refused as one, a quoted value ends at its first closing quote and with its line, an unquoted `#` starts a comment only after whitespace, a repeated key keeps its place and takes the last value — checked, with `withoutEdgeLineBreaks` and the reference pattern, against 143 cases answered by Coolify's own PHP (`tests/deploy-core-worker/fixtures/environment-variables`, with the script that made them); the save follows `handleBulkSubmit` and `updateOrder`: both texts are parsed and their keys normalized first (PHP's messages, the preview's behind "Preview deployments: "), then per set the variables the text no longer has are deleted (the `SERVICE_FQDN_`/`SERVICE_URL_`/`SERVICE_NAME_` ones stay), every copy of a key the view can edit — not locked, not multiline, a value the format can hold — takes the text's value, its inline comment when there is one and the flags of its `# coolify:` line, a key without a row is created (magic keys are skipped), each key's first row takes its line number as order, and the answer is "Environment variables updated." only when a row was written or deleted. `real_value` is a pure function over the shared variables the caller looked up (`deploy.shared_environment_variables` by scope, its own SQL since DP-50 is not in this tree; values under `shared_environment_variables.value`): every occurrence of a reference is filled in, one reference after the other, a locked shared variable only for a deployment, never for display or copy; `resolveReferencedValue` (`$OTHER`) feeds the copy. Rules and messages: `environmentVariableKeyRules()` with its two messages, `nullable|string|max:256` for the comment, `required|boolean` for the flags with Add's attribute names ("The multiline field …") and Show's derived ones ("The is multiline field …"), "Environment variable already exists." (before validation, as `Show::submit` orders it), "Required environment variables cannot be empty.", "The value cannot contain a NUL character.", "Duplicate environment variable key after normalization: X.", and the toasts word for word. Page, hand-ported from the Blade views inside the application's configuration frame: `/project/{uuid}/environment/{env_uuid}/application/{app_uuid}/environment-variables` — the card with its Developer view / Normal view button, the order and build secrets listboxes, "Include Freeploy default variables" and "Environment values in logs"; the toolbar (search, the filter menu with Managed / User-defined / Buildtime / Runtime / Multiline / Literal and the environment, Sort below 769px, Add); the table with the Production and Preview deployments groups, each row's lock glyph, comment, Required badge, Managed / Literal / Multiline / Buildtime / Runtime marks, copy button and edit dialog (name, comment, value — hidden, locked with its replacement field, single line with the `{{` suggestions or multiline —, resolved value, the four listboxes, the build warning of `EnvironmentVariableAnalyzer`, Delete with the typed name, Lock, Update variable), the "New Environment Variable" dialog, and the developer view's note, two textareas and unsaved bar. **Not checked in a browser or on stage yet**: the page is type-checked, linted and built, and the API is covered by tests. **Nothing is written to a server**: the .env file, build arguments and BuildKit secrets of a deployment are the deployment job's (W3), so the four switches, `is_literal`, `is_multiline`, `is_buildtime` and `is_runtime` are stored and shown and nothing reads them yet; the escaping of a value (`uses_legacy_escaping`, the "Old escaping" callout, `preserve_compose_null`) comes with it. Not ported: everything Compose — the hard-coded variables of a Compose file (`ShowHardcoded`), "Used by Compose", Compose defaults and "Use Compose default", the service filter, refusing to rename or delete a variable a Compose file uses (`isEnvironmentVariableUsedInDockerCompose` needs a YAML parser; no Compose application can be created yet), the generated magic variable that gets no preview copy; the secret manager (the `vault` scope, `secret-manager-links`, remote secret references); services and databases (the store, the service and the handler take the resource type; only `application` has an entry) with the Redis credential rows; the audit trail, `configurationChanged` and the configuration checker; `/api/v1/applications/{uuid}/envs` (Coolify's own JSON API) is DP9. Departures: authorization is the org-wide `deploy.resource.read`/`write` — Coolify's `viewSensitive` level between read and write does not exist, so reading a value needs `write` (`canViewValues` equals `canManage`; a reader sees the rows, "Hidden (only admins can view)" in the dialog and in the developer view's lines, and no copy button), and `authorizeAddedSharedVariableReferences` has nobody to refuse (whoever can save can read every shared variable), nor are there per-project access grants; the whole list is loaded once and searched, filtered, sorted and paged in the browser, where Coolify asks the server for each page (no 300 ms debounce, no loading overlay, no skeleton while it loads; the name sort compares code units, not the database's collation), the page size is remembered under `freeploy.page-size.environment-variables`, and the footer is the existing `<x-client-pagination>` port ("1-10 of 23" with a hyphen, where `<x-table-pagination>` writes a dash); the bulk save is one transaction, so an error changes nothing, where Coolify keeps what it saved before the error; `PATCH` is partial (a field left out keeps its value, where Livewire always sends every property), a value that is not a string, a number or null is refused with Laravel's `string` message and a switch that is not a boolean with Laravel's `boolean` message (Livewire fails on the property's type); the three Livewire actions of the switches are one `PATCH`, whose toast is "Log redaction setting updated." when that switch is the only one sent; a duplicate answers 409 and a missing variable 404; the Required badge shows for every required variable (Coolify narrows it to the empty ones once a row's dialog has loaded its value; the list leads with the empty ones either way); the New Environment Variable dialog stays open with its field errors (Coolify closes it on the click); `All::submit` re-applies the order of the developer view text a session opened earlier after every add, which a stateless API does not do; the `# coolify:` line of the developer view is kept as Coolify writes it (a text copied from Coolify reads the same), as are the `COOLIFY_*` names in the default variables' helper and the setting's column name, while its label reads "Include Freeploy default variables" and the edge-space hint "Freeploy keeps the space."; the value field's "Add … variables" link and the shared variable pages are DP-50's (not ported in this tree), and a `server` shared variable cannot be stored yet: the folded schema's `shared_environment_variables_type_check` allows only team, project and environment (Coolify's 2025_12_24 migration adds `server`), which this task found and left to DP-50; `Show::submit` does not refuse a magic variable (only the view hides its button) and neither does the API; `Str::trim`'s list of invisible characters is written out from Laravel's source by hand (Coolify's checkout has no `vendor` to run it); Livewire's string trimming is not assumed; helper texts are plain text.
- **Container labels (DP-53).** The label generation, HTTP Basic Authentication and the two container-label settings DP-49 deferred, without SSH and without a migration (the columns are in `500_deploy_core`). **The generators** are pure TypeScript in `apps/deploy-core-worker/src/labels/`: `labels.ts` ports `fqdnLabelsForTraefik`, `fqdnLabelsForCaddy`, `generateLabelsApplication` (production and pull-request previews, "exact labels" for the server's proxy or Traefik's then Caddy's), `defaultLabels`, `isNoindexDomain`, `caddySiteAddress`, `traefikHostRulesWithoutIpv6Brackets`, `escapeDollarSign`, and from `Server`: `proxyType`, `traefikCertificateResolver`, `hasPendingProxyConfiguration`, `caddyDockerProxyImageVersion` and the `caddySupports…` checks; `custom-labels.ts` ports `isBase64Encoded`, `decodeBase64EncodedLabels` and `Application::parseContainerLabels`. Spatie's `Url::fromString` is read through DP-49's `parseUrl`; `Collection::sort()` is a byte-wise sort (PHP compares bytes, JavaScript UTF-16 units); PHP's truthiness of ports (`""`, `"0"`) and of a Collection (an empty `serviceLabels` still switches Caddy's ingress network) is kept. **The label keys and values are Coolify's, unchanged** — `coolify.managed=true`, `coolify.applicationUuid=…`, `coolify.version=4.7.6` (the pinned release), `caddy_ingress_network=…`, `traefik.http.routers.https-0-<uuid>…`, the `gzip-no-media` and `redirect-to-https` middlewares — also where the pages say "Freeploy": they are written to the user's servers and read back by the proxies and by Coolify-compatible tooling. **Verification is option (a): Coolify's own PHP.** `composer install --no-dev --no-scripts --ignore-platform-reqs` works in a copy of the pinned checkout, and the Laravel application boots locally (PHP 8.3.6, Laravel 12.72.0) with no database; `tests/deploy-core-worker/fixtures/labels/emit.php` calls the real functions — the models built in memory with their relations set, `save()` a no-op — over `cases.json` and writes `goldens.json` (the README there has the exact command). 185 cases, all matched byte for byte by `labels.test.ts`: 50 `fqdnLabelsForTraefik`, 35 `fqdnLabelsForCaddy`, 40 `generateLabelsApplication`, 7 `parseContainerLabels`, 6 `defaultLabels`, 11 `decodeBase64EncodedLabels`, 7 `isBase64Encoded`, 15 for the small helpers, and 14 that run `General::rules()`, `messages()` and `$validationAttributes` through Laravel's validator for the label fields; two Caddy cases are Coolify throwing on an invalid URL, which the port also does with Spatie's message. A mutation (no final sort, one bcrypt round fewer) fails 120 tests. **bcrypt**: Coolify hashes the password with `password_hash(…, PASSWORD_BCRYPT, ['cost' => …])`, so `labels/bcrypt.ts` is a bcrypt (`$2y$`, written from the specification; Blowfish's tables are the hexadecimal digits of pi, computed once into `blowfish-pi.ts`) whose only random input, the salt, is a parameter: the test takes the salt out of every hash in the goldens and the port must return PHP's hash, which it does for costs 4, 5, 10 and 12, a multibyte password and one longer than 72 bytes. No dependency was added. **Wiring**, as `General` does it: `GET …/deploy/applications/{uuid}` now also carries `customLabels`, `isHttpBasicAuthEnabled`, `httpBasicAuthUsername`, `httpBasicAuthPassword`, `httpBasicAuthHashAlgorithm`, `httpBasicAuthBcryptCost`, the two Argon2id costs, `supportsArgon2id` and `proxyType`; like `mount()`, it rewrites `custom_labels` as base64 of the text (plain and multiply-encoded rows are read) and, for someone who may update, fills an application that has none with the generated labels when labels are managed and the server's proxy is not `NONE`. The labels text and the password are null for a reader (`syncData`'s `$canViewSecrets`). `PATCH` follows `submit()`'s order: the settings and authentication fields are stored (the password with the per-org cipher, bound to `applications.http_basic_auth_password`; only changed columns are written), then managed labels are regenerated when the application has domains (`checkFqdns`) or when the exposed ports or the escape setting changed, hand-written labels are stored as given, an empty hand-written text on a server with a proxy starts from the generated labels, and `custom_labels = base64_encode(text)` — an application without domains keeps its stored labels unless ports or escaping change, which is Coolify's behaviour, not a simplification. `PATCH` with `instantSave: true` is `General::instantSave` (the listboxes: method, hashing algorithm and costs, label management, special characters; "Settings saved."; managed labels regenerated), and `POST …/applications/{uuid}/labels/reset` is `resetDefaultLabels(true)`; both need `deploy.resource.write`, a denial 404. Rules and messages are Laravel's own output for `General::rules()`: "The Container Label Escape setting must be true or false.", "The HTTP Basic Auth setting is required.", "The http basic auth password field must not be greater than 72 characters.", "The http basic auth bcrypt cost field must be at least 4.", "The selected http basic auth hash algorithm is invalid.", and `syncData`'s "Argon2id needs a server that runs the Caddy proxy, version 2.11 or newer." (422). New code is in new files (`labels/*`, `applications/labels-{service,validation}.ts`, `handlers/application-labels.ts`, `@saas/db/deploy`'s `application-labels.ts` with `findApplicationLabelSource`, `updateApplicationLabels`, `updateApplicationLabelSettings`, tested on PGlite with row-level security); `handlers/applications.ts` dispatches to them, `ApplicationContext` gained an optional `labels`, and the applications route accepts a third path segment. **Page**: General's Authentication sub-section (the method, username and password, "Password hashing" with the algorithm and cost; the "Authentication is managed through labels" notice while labels are manual) and Container's Labels sub-section (label management, special characters, "Active labels" with "Reset to defaults" behind Coolify's confirmation — type the application URL — and the labels field, read-only while managed) are hand-ported from the Blade view, copy word for word with "Coolify" reading "Freeploy"; the listbox component gained disabled options for Argon2id. **Departures.** (1) The labels field is a plain textarea, not the Monaco editor. (2) While labels are managed, a `customLabels` in the body is ignored; Coolify stores whatever the Livewire property holds, which its read-only field never changes. (3) A `customLabels` that is not a string or null is a field error ("The Custom labels field must be a string."); Coolify's rule is only `nullable` and a list then fails in `base64_encode()`. (4) An instant save reads only the listbox fields and leaves other unsaved edits in the form; Coolify sends the whole form and, when the method or the hashing changed, saves all of it. (5) The whole `PATCH` is one transaction: a domain conflict (409) or the Argon2id refusal stores nothing, where Coolify has already saved the settings row. (6) A missing field keeps its stored value (the API is a PATCH; Livewire always sends every property). (7) `Str::slug` in `defaultLabels` is exact for ASCII and Latin letters with diacritics (goldens include `ß`, `æ`, `ø`, `ł`); a character of another script, which Coolify transliterates through portable-ascii's tables, is dropped. **Deferred.** Argon2id: the label format is ported and checked (the goldens' hash is handed to the port), but there is no Argon2id implementation, and no server can reach it — `Server::configuredCaddyProxyImage()` parses the saved proxy configuration (YAML), which Freeploy does not save until the proxy pages are ported, so `caddySupportsArgon2idBasicAuth`, `basic_auth` and `log_append` are off as on a Coolify server whose configuration cannot be read (`basicauth`, bcrypt). The deployment job's use of the labels (`generate_compose_file`: dropping `coolify.*` from custom labels, `useDestinationNetworkInCaddyLabels`, the escape setting through `escapeDollarSign`, merging `defaultLabels`) — the pieces are ported, nothing calls them yet; `previewCustomLabels`, `maintenanceProxyLabels`; `generate_unique_uuid` is ported with an injected uuid but has no golden (it is random); `generateServiceSpecificFqdns` (not called by `generateLabelsApplication`) and `convertDockerRunToCompose`; regenerating labels from the Domains and Advanced pages (`setRedirect`, the gzip, strip-prefix and force-https toggles, DP-52) and from Coolify's JSON API (`applyApplicationSettings`); a `scheme:rest` domain without `//`, which Coolify's validation never stores; `General`'s `configurationChanged` event and the audit trail. `tools/parity/commands` is untouched: its `goldens.json` is re-emitted in the reference container by CI and may not be edited by hand, and its emitter passes literal arguments, where these functions take Collections and models.
- **Applications from a Dockerfile, a public Git repository and Docker Compose (DP-54).** Two more ways to create an application, and General's fields of the build packs, without SSH, over `deploy.applications`, `deploy.application_settings` and `deploy.environment_variables` (no migration): `POST /v1/organizations/{org}/deploy/applications` now also takes `type: "dockerfile"` (`SimpleDockerfile::submit`: `dockerfile`) and `type: "public"` (`PublicGitRepository::submit`: `repository_url`, `git_branch`, `build_pack`, `port`, `isStatic`, `publish_directory`, `base_directory`, `docker_compose_location`, `selectedDockerfile`), `POST …/applications/git/check` is `loadBranch()` ("Check repository") and `POST …/applications/git/scan` is `detectRepository()` (Smart Scan); `GET`/`PATCH …/applications/{uuid}` carry a `build` object beside `application` with the build pack's fields; everything stays inside `withOrg` with `deploy.resource.write` (reads: `read`), a denial 404; Select's catalogue marks `public` and `dockerfile` creatable. Nothing is deployed and no deployment is queued. **What is stored.** From a Dockerfile, as `SimpleDockerfile::submit` and the model hooks do: name `dockerfile-<uuid>`, `repository_project_id` 0, `git_repository` `coollabsio/coolify`, `git_branch` `main` (Coolify's placeholders), `build_pack` `dockerfile`, the file's text in `dockerfile`, `ports_exposes` the number after the first `EXPOSE` (`get_port_from_dockerfile`, else 80), `health_check_enabled` false, `source_id` 0 with `source_type` `App\Models\GithubApp`, the four encrypted webhook secrets, `compose_parsing_version` `5`, `generateUrl()`'s domain, a settings row; then `parseHealthcheckFromDockerfile(isInit: true)` — the `HEALTHCHECK` of the stage Docker builds (`dockerfileHealthcheckInstruction`, with `FROM` chains, `# escape=` and `HEALTHCHECK NONE`) sets interval, timeout, start period and retries (`dockerfileHealthcheckOptions`, Go durations as whole seconds) and `custom_healthcheck_found`. From a public Git repository: the URL is rewritten as `loadBranch()` does (an SSH URL reads as https, `.git` is added to any http(s) URL and taken off a github.com one) and read as `getGitSource()` does; a github.com repository is stored as `owner/name` with the public GitHub source and named by `generate_application_name()` (`<repo>:<branch>-<id>`), any other host as its whole URL with no source and a `generate_random_name()`; `ports_exposes` is the form's port, `base_directory`, `publish_directory`, `dockerfile_location` (Dockerfile build pack with a typed path only) and `docker_compose_location` (Compose only) go through the model's mutators and the saving hook's trim, `health_check_enabled` is false for the Dockerfile build pack, `repository_project_id` stays null, `is_static` goes on the settings row (false for any build pack but Nixpacks and Railpack, as `updatedBuildPack()` forces), every build pack gets `generateUrl()`'s domain (a Compose application too, as in Coolify), and Nixpacks gets the created hook's `NIXPACKS_NODE_VERSION` = `22` (build-time, not runtime) with the preview copy `EnvironmentVariable`'s own created hook makes, both values encrypted with the per-org cipher bound to `environment_variables.value`, `version` DP-51's `ENVIRONMENT_VARIABLE_VERSION`. **The public GitHub source.** Coolify seeds a `GithubApp` with id 0 ("Public GitHub", team 0) for the whole instance; `deploy.github_apps` rows each belong to one org and its identity starts at 1, so no row is seeded and no schema is added: `source_id` 0 with `source_type` `App\Models\GithubApp` — the two values Coolify stores — *is* the public source, resolved in code by `PUBLIC_GITHUB_SOURCE` (`@saas/db/deploy`: api `https://api.github.com`, html `https://github.com`, `is_public`); later code that follows `source` must treat that pair as this constant before it looks for a row. The public GitLab source is not used by any creation path here. **Check repository.** `ValidGitRepositoryUrl` and `ValidGitBranch` are ported rule by rule with their messages ("Invalid repository URL: The repository url contains invalid characters.", "… cannot point to internal hosts." for localhost, loopback and PHP's reserved ranges, …). For github.com the branch is asked of `https://api.github.com/repos/{owner}/{name}/branches/{branch}` as `githubApi()` does for a public source (no token, `Accept: application/vnd.github.v3+json`, a 5xx or a dropped connection tried three times one and two seconds apart) through an injectable `fetch` — tests never touch the network; a branch like `feature/x/apps/web` from a `…/tree/…` URL is cut back at each `/` until GitHub knows it and the rest is the base directory; the answer carries the rate limit (`Y-M-d H:i:s`, as Coolify formats it). When GitHub refuses, Coolify's three ways are kept: with `rate_limit_remaining` 0 — the component's starting value, so on every first check — the branch is taken as found; otherwise `main` is tried as `master`; otherwise the error is `githubApi()`'s message. The API keeps no component state, so the form sends back the `rate_limit_remaining` of its last check. Any other host is not asked (`branchFound` at once), a tangled URL is on `master`. **General.** `PATCH` validates the build fields with General's `rules()`, `messages()` and `$validationAttributes` word for word — build pack ("The Build Pack field is required."), static image (`StaticImageTypes`), base and publish directory (`DIRECTORY_PATH_PATTERN`), install, build and start command (shell-safe, at most 1000 characters, only when changed: `changedCommandRules`), Dockerfile, its location (`FILE_PATH_PATTERN`) and build target, Compose location and the two custom Compose commands, watch paths, custom Nginx configuration, and the settings static, SPA, build server and preserve repository ("The Static setting must be true or false.") — together with DP-49's fields in one request and one transaction, and saves through the model's mutators and hooks: directories without a trailing slash, commands trimmed, watch paths one pattern per line without a leading `/`, the Nginx configuration base64-encoded (empty is null), `dockerfile_location` `/Dockerfile` for the Dockerfile build pack. A changed build pack is `updatedBuildPack()` then `submit()`: Static sets `ports_exposes` 80 and the default static Nginx configuration (`defaultNginxConfiguration`), leaving Nixpacks/Railpack ends `is_static`, and the saving hook's cleanup runs — leaving Dockerfile clears `dockerfile`, its location, target and `custom_healthcheck_found`; leaving Docker Compose clears `docker_compose_raw`, `docker_compose_domains` and deletes the application's `SERVICE_FQDN_*` / `SERVICE_URL_*` variables. A changed Dockerfile re-reads its `HEALTHCHECK` (reset to 5/5/10/5 when it is gone) and gives `ports_exposes` its `EXPOSE` port when none is set. `{ instantSave: true }` is `instantSave()`, one rule with DP-53's label and authentication listboxes — a request may carry both kinds, they are validated together, the build settings are saved first and the answer comes once, with `application` and `build` — ("Settings saved.": the site type — `siteType` `dynamic`/`static`/`spa` —, the builder, preserve repository; a changed SPA setting regenerates the Nginx configuration, preserve repository switched off clears `is_based_on_git` on the application's file storages), `{ generateNginxConfiguration: "static" | "spa" }` is that action ("Nginx configuration generated."). An image name another application of the org already builds is refused on `dockerRegistryImageName` with `sharedImageNameMessage()` (`otherApplicationBuildingSameImage`); the image name is required only for a Docker image application. The read hides commands, the Dockerfile, the Compose file and the Nginx configuration from who cannot update (`canViewSecrets`) and carries `gitBased`, `hasDockerfile`, `publicRepository`, `supportsWatchPaths`, `couldSetBuildCommands`, `siteType`, `hasBuildServers` and the two Compose command previews (`injectDockerComposeFlags`). **Not available yet** (the SSH executor): Smart Scan — `RepositoryDetector` clones the repository on the server — answers 501 "Scanning the repository needs the SSH executor, which is not available yet." after `detectRepository()`'s own checks (branch, base directory, "Destination not found."), so nothing is ever detected: no suggested build pack, no detected Dockerfiles, Compose files or ports, no env file import (`importDetectedEnvironmentVariables`); and loading a Compose file from the repository (`Application::loadComposeFile`) — a `PATCH` that changes the Compose location or the base directory of a Compose application answers 501 "Loading the Compose file from the repository needs the SSH executor, which is not available yet." and stores nothing (Coolify restores the old paths when the load fails), the first load on opening the page does not run, so `docker_compose_raw` stays null and General says so. **Cut: the New Resource page's "Docker Compose" type.** In Coolify `New\DockerCompose` does not create an application: it stores a `Service` and runs `$service->parse(isNew: true)` over the pasted YAML (after `parseDockerComposeYaml` and `validateDockerComposeForInjection`), which is the Compose parser of DP8 — there is no YAML parser in the Worker and none may be added, and a `services` row without its parsed applications and databases would be a broken resource with no page to open. `type: "dockercompose"` answers 501 "Creating a service from a Docker Compose file needs the Compose parser, which is not available yet.", the type stays not creatable in the catalogue and its form is not ported; the pinned Coolify has no `create_dockercompose_application` API either. A Docker Compose *application* is created through the public Git repository form with the Docker Compose build pack. Pages, hand-ported from the Blade views: on `/project/{uuid}/environment/{env_uuid}/new` the Dockerfile form (`?type=dockerfile`) and the Public Git Repository form (`?type=public`: Check repository, then the build configuration with the rate limit callout, the Smart Scan panel, branch, build pack, output type, port, publish directory, the Dockerfile path, `<x-forms.repository-paths>` with its resolved path and two hints, the Nixpacks deprecation callout), and on the application's General page the "Build & deploy" section of a built application (build strategy, web server, site type, repository paths, publish directory, watch paths, install/build/start commands, build stage target, builder selection, preserve repository, the Compose commands with their previews, the Nginx configuration with "Generate default", the raw Compose file read-only, the Dockerfile, the image registry, and the container names of the deployment commands for Compose); a Compose application hides Internal access and the Container section and shows no domain on the Public access card, as the view does. **Not checked in a browser or on stage yet**: the pages are type-checked, linted and built, and the API is covered by tests. Departures: the build pack must be one of the listbox's five on creation and when it changes in General (Coolify's rule is only `required`; the stored one always passes); `dockerComposeRaw` and `dockerCompose` are not accepted by `PATCH` (read-only in the view; Coolify lets a forged request change the raw file after `validateDockerComposeForInjection`, which needs the parser) and `docker_compose` (the generated file) is never returned; General's `gitRepository`, `gitBranch` and `gitCommitSha` rules are not applied — the view edits them on the Git Source page, not ported (DP6) — and the per-service domains of a Compose application (`parsedServiceDomains`, `generateDomain`, the "Reload compose" action, "Show deployable compose", the label-escape checkbox of the Compose block) wait for the parser; `mount()`'s `parse()` and its writes are not run; the Compose build command preview does not inject build arguments (they come from the environment variables, DP-51); the builder selection's helper drops the fallback sentence (a team setting, `is_build_server_fallback_enabled`, Freeploy does not have) and its documentation link, `hasBuildServers` counts usable build servers (no GitHub runner exclusion), and the registry image is marked required for Swarm or a build server only (`additional_servers` and `canBuildApplications()` are not ported); every `PATCH` is partial, a setting sent with `instantSave` is saved the same way without it, and the SPA regeneration and the file storage reset happen only with `instantSave` as in Coolify; a blank build target or Compose command is stored as null (Livewire stores the empty string) and a command equal to the stored one as text is not written; the install, build, start and custom Compose commands are `VARCHAR(255)` in the folded schema while Coolify's rule allows 1000 characters — a longer one fails in the database (503) as it would with that schema in Coolify; the creation API takes the page's route and query values in the body and re-derives the repository, host and source from `repository_url` on submit (the component keeps them from the check), so the check is not required before creating; GitHub's error message has line breaks where Coolify's has `<br>`, and the request sends `User-Agent: Freeploy` (GitHub refuses a request without one; Laravel's client always sends its own); `FILTER_VALIDATE_URL` and PHP's reserved IP ranges are re-implemented for these URLs, `rawurldecode` byte-wise; the retry waits use the Worker's timer; `get_port_from_dockerfile` returns PHP's `(int)` of what follows `EXPOSE` (0 for `$PORT`), treated as no port everywhere as in Coolify; Smart Scan's panel only ever shows "Not scanned yet" and the Dockerfile of the Dockerfile build pack is a plain field instead of the searchable listbox of detected files; the detected Compose file listbox and the env import modal are not ported; the repository URL helper and the other HTML helpers are plain text, "Coolify Examples" keeps its name (it is the repository's); Monaco is a plain textarea for the Dockerfile, the Nginx configuration and the Compose file; "Preserve repository" is a native checkbox with its helper as a title; the `apache:alpine` web server option (disabled in Coolify) and the disabled "No build servers connected" option's disabled state are not drawn (choosing it does nothing); the configuration menu still shows Git Source for an application created from a Dockerfile (`git_based()` is false there; the menu only knows the build pack) and its page is not ported; a creation with a runner that has no build store answers 503; the audit trail and `configurationChanged` are not ported; "Coolify" in the copy reads "Freeploy".
- **Application storage, tags, resource operations and danger zone (DP-55).** Four more of an application's configuration pages, without SSH, over `deploy.local_persistent_volumes`, `deploy.local_file_volumes`, `deploy.tags`, `deploy.taggables` and the application's own rows: `GET`/`POST …/deploy/applications/{uuid}/storages` (POST is one of the four "Add mount" forms, named by `type`: `volume`, `file`, `host-file`, `directory`, with Coolify's property names as fields), `PATCH`/`DELETE …/storages/volumes/{id}`, `PATCH`/`DELETE …/storages/files/{id}` (PATCH is one of FileStorage's actions, named by `action`), `GET`/`POST …/tags`, `POST`/`DELETE …/tags/{id}`, `GET …/resource-operations`, `POST …/resource-operations/clone` and `…/move`, `GET …/danger` and `DELETE …/applications/{uuid}` with Coolify's API query fields (`delete_volumes`, `delete_connected_networks`, `delete_configurations`, `docker_cleanup`, `delete_backups`; a DELETE's options are in the query because the edge forwards no body with it). All of it is a new handler (`handlers/application-resources.ts`, dispatched by one line of `handleApplications`; the applications route now takes four segments below the collection) inside `withOrg`, policy `deploy.resource.read`/`write` with 404 on denial, reads carrying `canUpdate`, `canDeploy` and `canDelete`. The SQL is new files of `@saas/db/deploy` (`storages.ts`, `tags.ts`, `resource-operations.ts`, `application-delete.ts`); volumes, mounts and tags are morph rows and every function takes the owner's morph class, so services and databases reuse them. **One migration, `610_deploy_tags_org_name_unique`**: the schema fold kept Coolify's first, global unique index on `tags.name` (Coolify dropped it in `2024_07_05_120217` and made the name unique per team in `2026_07_07_113248`), so a tag name one org used was refused to every other org; 610 drops it and adds `(org_id, name)`. Ported from `app/Livewire/Project/Service/{Storage,FileStorage}.php`, `app/Livewire/Project/Shared/Storages/All.php`, `app/Livewire/Project/Shared/{Tags,ResourceOperations,Danger}.php`, `app/Models/{LocalPersistentVolume,LocalFileVolume,Tag}.php`, `clone_application()`, `DeleteResourceJob` and the path helpers of `bootstrap/helpers/shared.php`, with Coolify's and Laravel's messages word for word. **Persistent Storage.** A volume is a row (it takes effect at the next deployment): added as `<application uuid>-<name>` with `volumeNameRules()` and the mount path as the mutator stores it, edited with `DIRECTORY_PATH_PATTERN` and the PR suffix switch — a new name answers 409 with `pendingRename` until `renameConfirmed`, as the "Change the volume name?" dialog — and deleted unless it has a backup schedule or is a SQLite database's. File mounts follow `validateFileMountPath`/`confineFileMountPath`/`resolveHostPath`/`validateHostFileMountPath` (`applications/storage-paths.ts`), `content` is an `encrypted` cast and is stored with the org's key bound to `local_file_volumes.content`, sent only to who may update, replaced by the too-large placeholder above 5 MB. Coolify's two unique indexes (`name` per resource for volumes, `mount_path` per resource for mounts) are not in the folded schema; the duplicate is checked before the write and answered as `handleError()` does: 422 "Duplicate entry found. Please use a different name." **What needs the SSH executor, in Coolify's own order of row and command**: a managed file mount without a source path stores the row and the answer carries Coolify's success message plus `unavailable` "Writing the file to the server needs the SSH executor, which is not available yet." (the created hook's `ServerStorageSaveJob`); a file mount with a source path and a directory mount inside the application's directory are checked on the server before the row (`assertHostPathOnServer`), so they answer 501 "Checking the path on the server needs …" and store nothing — a directory elsewhere on the host and a host file mount are rows only, as in Coolify; saving a file's content (`submit()`: the row, the write, the row put back when the write fails) answers 501 and stores nothing, while `instantSave` (the content and the two switches, no server) is stored; converting a mount and "Load from server" answer 501; deleting a mount with `permanently_delete` on a mount inside the application's directory answers 501 and keeps the row (the dialog's box is checked by default, as in Coolify, so removing only the mount means unchecking it), and deleting a volume with `deleteDockerVolume` likewise. **Tags.** `POST …/tags { newTags }` adds each space-separated name on its own — an existing tag of the org is attached, a new name creates it (`strip_tags`, lower case) — and answers the names it skipped as `errors` (too short, already attached) in Coolify's words; `POST`/`DELETE …/tags/{id}` attach and detach one of the org's tags, the tag itself staying. **Resource Operations.** Move sets the application's environment (any environment of the org). Clone is `clone_application()` as rows: the application's row column for column (the columns are read from the catalogue, so a new column is copied like the rest) with a new uuid, the name `clone-of-<Str::limit(name, 20)>-<uuid>`, status `exited`, the chosen environment and destination, and — when the labels are read-only and the server has a proxy — a domain generated again for the new server, with the container labels generated again for it by DP-53's generator (`generateLabelsApplication()` for the clone, stored as base64); then its settings row, tags, scheduled tasks, volume mounts under the clone's names, file and directory mounts, and environment variables (production and preview). Encrypted columns are copied as stored: a value is bound to its org and column (`crypto.ts`), not to its row, so the copies decrypt as the source does.
